`simulate_season()`'s signature changed from `tillage_doy, tillage_boost_days, tillage_boost_factor` to `tillage_doy, tillage_implement, tillage_boost_days` -- `tillage_implement` selects a real named implement from `TILLAGE_IMPLEMENTS` and raises `ValueError` for an unrecognized name. Two real effects now, split by how directly each is grounded in real data: (1) the new genuine moisture-mixing call, fired once on `tillage_doy` itself; (2) the existing background-nitrogen-mineralization boost (still a disclosed placeholder for the real, undisclosed decomposition mechanism -- see above) is now sized by `1 + mixing_efficiency`, a real per-implement number, instead of the old arbitrary caller-supplied `2.0`. Verified four ways in plain CPython against real Rock Springs weather (2012): an unrecognized implement name raises `ValueError`; `Plow_moldboard` (depth=0.18m, mixing_efficiency=0.8, the most aggressive real implement in the catalog) measurably raises yield at a nitrogen-limiting rate (50 kg N/ha: 4.85 -> 5.27 Mg/ha in the embedded-copy test); the same implement has ~no effect at a non-limiting rate (650 kg N/ha: 10.4987 -> 10.4989 Mg/ha); and a zero-mixing implement (`Fert_applic_surface_broadcast`) produces no change at all. Regression-verified with both existing harnesses (`run_validation.py`, `run_validation_rotation2.py`) reproducing the exact previously-documented correlation numbers for all four already-validated crops with `tillage_doy=None` (the default, unaffected) -- confirming the redesign is fully backward-compatible. Ported into `engine-demo.html`'s embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE` copy and verified there too, the same way this file always verifies Pyodide-dependent features: extracted the exact embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE`/`DEMO_GLUE_SOURCE`/weather-data strings verbatim from the live HTML and ran all four tests above directly in CPython against them, additionally reconfirming the untouched default path still reproduces the exact previously-documented reference number (2012 corn @ 650 kg N/ha, 10.4987 Mg/ha).

Tillage UI wired into the field-comparison and frontier panels (2026-09-18, same day, per Matt's direct "Now wire tillage into the field-comparison and frontier panels' UI" -- a scoped exception to the "no UI wiring yet" stance above, not a reversal of it; the other four mechanisms and the rotation/year-sweep panels stay untouched). A shared `TILLAGE_OPTIONS` array (five entries: No tillage / field cultivator / chisel plow / disk / moldboard plow, spanning the real no-till-to-conventional intensity spectrum farmers actually discuss) and a `buildTillageFieldHTML()` template function back one `<select>` in the "How much does nitrogen matter?" panel and one shared (not per-field) `<select>` in "Pick a field and run a simulation" -- shared across Field A/B deliberately, so tillage doesn't become a second confound alongside the location comparison that panel exists to isolate. Only five of the real 86 `TILLAGE_IMPLEMENTS` are exposed (most of the 86 are harvesters/planters, not tillage passes, and exposing all 86 would compound the exact Frankenstein problem Matt flagged) -- chosen to span real-world tillage intensity, not picked arbitrarily. Applied at planting (`tillage_doy = plant_doy`) in both panels: the engine's weather rows for a season start at planting itself, so an earlier real-world pre-plant pass has no day inside the simulated window to land on -- stated as a disclosed simplification in the nitrogen-sweep panel's own copy, not a claim that real tillage always happens on planting day. "No tillage" (the default in both selects) sets `tillage_doy=None, tillage_implement=None`, reproducing each panel's exact pre-tillage-control Python byte-for-byte.

Verified two ways, the same split this file always uses for anything Pyodide-dependent: (1) the real embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE`/`DEMO_GLUE_SOURCE`/weather-data strings extracted verbatim and driven in plain CPython with both call sites' exact new Python bodies -- confirmed the no-tillage default reproduces the exact prior reference number (2012 corn @ 650 kg N/ha, 10.4987 Mg/ha) in the nitrogen-sweep panel's code path; confirmed `Plow_moldboard` measurably raises yield at a nitrogen-limiting rate in both the nitrogen-sweep panel's code path (N=50: 4.85 -> 5.27 Mg/ha) and the field-comparison panel's code path (N=150 at Rock Springs' own soil: 8.35 -> 8.66 Mg/ha); confirmed an unrecognized implement name still raises `ValueError` (unreachable from the UI itself, since the select only offers real catalog keys, but the engine's own guard is unchanged). (2) Headless Chromium with a mocked `pyodide` confirmed both selects render the expected five options, clicking each panel's run button with the default selection sets an empty-string tillage global and takes the `tillage_doy=None` code branch, selecting "Moldboard plow" sets the correct implement-name global and takes the `tillage_doy=(...if...else None)` branch, and the field-comparison panel's result note correctly names the selected tillage practice in its own text (e.g. "Ran Field A for 2012 (moldboard plow (conventional, most aggressive))").

Tillage UI extended to the rotation panel (2026-09-18, same day, per Matt's direct "wire tillage into the rotation panel too"): the same `TILLAGE_OPTIONS`/`buildTillageFieldHTML()` machinery now backs a third `<select>` in "Simulate a rotation: corn -> winter cover crop -> soybean," scoped to the corn phase only -- the point in a real corn/cover/soybean rotation where a primary tillage pass typically happens, unlike the cover crop (usually drilled with minimal disturbance) or the soybean phase, both left untouched by this control. The panel's own copy now states that scoping explicitly rather than leaving a student to wonder why one dropdown affects three phases or none of them. `tillage_doy`/`tillage_implement` are passed only into the corn-phase `simulate_season()` call; the cover-crop and soybean calls are unchanged. The corn row of the results table now names the selected practice (e.g. "Corn (2012, planted DOY 111, moldboard plow (conventional, most aggressive))"), the same pattern the field-comparison panel's result note already uses.

Verified the same two ways as the other two panels: (1) the real embedded engine/glue/weather extracted verbatim and driven in plain CPython -- the no-tillage default reproduces the exact previously-documented rotation numbers in CLAUDE.md's own account (2012 corn N=150/cover N=30: cover-crop leaching 15.97 kg N/ha vs. 20.75 kg N/ha bare fallow), confirming this addition is byte-identical when unused; `Plow_moldboard` changes the corn phase's grain (7.86 -> 8.15 Mg/ha) while leaving the cover-crop and soybean phases' numbers completely unchanged, confirming the corn-only scoping actually holds in the engine, not just in the UI copy; the same implement measurably raises corn yield at a nitrogen-limiting rate (N=50: 4.85 -> 5.27 Mg/ha), consistent with the mechanism verified in the other two panels. (2) Headless Chromium with a mocked `pyodide` confirmed the select renders the same five options, the default sets an empty-string global with the `tillage_doy=None` branch, selecting "Moldboard plow" sets the implement-name global with the correct branch, and the corn row's displayed text names the selected practice.

Not yet done: "Run a season" and the year-sweep panel still have no tillage control -- both of Matt's tillage-UI requests so far (the field-comparison/frontier panels, then the rotation panel) named specific panels rather than asking for every panel at once, so this stays scoped rather than assumed to generalize. Tillage's real decomposition-boost mechanism (the actual six-pool `fT >= 1` factor, SI Sec. V) is still not implemented -- what's built and now exposed in three panels' UI is a disclosed stand-in for its practical effect, sized by a real number, not a reproduction of the real mechanism; that gap needs either the devs' meeting or a successful OMML extraction attempt to close. Injection fertilization (the volatilization-reduction angle) has no engine mechanism or UI at all yet, pending a disclosed volatilization formula from either source -- not started, per the earlier recommendation not to build ahead of a known formula.

Real GenericCrops.crop and till.txt uploaded and cross-checked (2026-09-21): Matt uploaded both files directly, asking whether each was the data being waited on. `GenericCrops.crop` (39 species) turned out to be byte-identical to the copy already sitting in this container's `/tmp/cycles-run/input/` from earlier work, and cross-checking it against three already-built crop dicts (`CORN`, `SOYBEAN`, `WHEAT`) confirmed every field matches exactly -- genuine, authentic data, not a different or updated version. The real disappointment: no `WinterRye` or `HairyVetch` entry exists anywhere in it (grepped case-insensitively for "rye" and "vetch," zero matches) -- Kemanian et al. 2024's SI Table SI.2 names both species with RUE/WUE numbers, but the actual released crop file doesn't carry a full parameter set for either. Matt's call when told this: no specific reason to chase winter rye or hairy vetch further without real data, so the disclosed-placeholder implementation stays exactly as it is (`WINTER_RYE` borrowing `WHEAT`'s temperature curve, `HAIRY_VETCH` borrowing `WHEAT`'s temperature curve and `SOYBEAN`'s nitrogen behavior) -- a real, better-grounded swap (`WinterPeas`/`WinterLentils`, genuine winter-hardy legumes in this same file, for `HAIRY_VETCH`'s temperature donor) was identified and offered but not built, since Matt de-prioritized the whole thread. `till.txt` was also uploaded and confirmed byte-identical to the copy already used to build `TILLAGE_IMPLEMENTS` -- cross-checked all 86 entries programmatically (depth_m and mixing_efficiency for every real implement) against the embedded dict in `cycles_engine_validate.py`: zero mismatches, zero missing, zero extras either direction. One real field neither upload changed anything about: each till.txt entry also carries a `SOIL_DISTURB_RATIO` not currently used anywhere in this engine -- noted in case a future mechanism needs it, not built toward speculatively.

`agricultural/investigation.html` created (2026-09-21), the first real guided-investigation tab shell for this unit, per Matt's "I like the proposed order and would like to see that and decide on tweaks" -- same discipline as anthropology/meteorology/engineering: get the tab sequence looked at and adjusted before investing further, not build all six tabs blind. Six tabs, the exact sequence proposed and discussed earlier this session: Watch a Season Grow, What Changes the Outcome?, Does Crop Choice Matter?, Nitrogen and Tillage (No Single Best Rate), Rotations and Cover Crops, Design Your Own Investigation. Tab 1 is built for real; Tabs 2-6 are explicit "Not built yet" stubs with a real paragraph each describing what will go there (the exact engineering-unit stub convention), not silently missing content.

Tab 1 ("Watch a Season Grow") reuses `engine-demo.html`'s own "Run a season" panel via iframe rather than duplicating its ~600KB embedded engine-and-weather blob into a second file -- the same architecture decision meteorology already made for its own sandbox (`embed=1`), applied here for the same reason. A new `?embed=run-season` query param on `engine-demo.html` hides its own `<header>`, the "Select your site" panel (unnecessary here since `currentSite` already defaults to Rock Springs with no click needed), and every panel except "Run a season" itself; any other or missing `embed` value leaves the page fully unchanged, same "ignore what isn't recognized" convention the meteorology deep-link params use. `#panel-select-site` and `#panel-run-season` ids were added to make the toggle addressable. One real ordering bug caught before it shipped, not after: the toggle script was first placed *before* the `<footer>` element in the HTML, so `document.querySelector("footer")` returned null at the time the script ran (the footer hadn't been parsed into the DOM yet) -- fixed by moving the script to after the footer, not by deferring it, since nothing else in the script depends on load timing.

Verified in headless Chromium against the real served files (this sandbox still can't reach the Pyodide CDN, the same standing caveat as every Pyodide-dependent feature in this project -- the actual live season chart hasn't been checked in a real browser): normal-mode load shows all 6 panels on `engine-demo.html`, `?embed=run-season` shows exactly 1 (header, select-site, and footer all hidden), and an unrecognized `embed` value behaves identically to no param at all. On `investigation.html` itself: all 6 tab labels render in order, clicking each tab activates exactly that one content div and no other, the iframe's `src` resolves to the real embed URL, and reading inside the iframe confirms the embed logic actually took effect there too (header/select-site hidden, run-season panel visible, its real `cropSelect`/`runBtn` elements present). Screenshots of Tab 1 and Tab 5 confirm the page reads cleanly and matches `engine-demo.html`'s own established visual language, reused directly (same CSS custom properties) for consistency within the unit.

Not yet done, deliberately: `index.html`'s splash card still points straight at `engine-demo.html`, not this new `investigation.html` -- this is a first look for Matt to react to and adjust before it's the thing students would land on, the same reason the footer states plainly "not yet linked from the splash page." The iframe's fixed 900px height is an estimate, not measured against a real loaded season chart (Pyodide can't load in this sandbox) -- may need adjusting once checked in a real browser. Tabs 2-6 are text descriptions only, no code -- each is real design intent carried over from this session's scoping conversation, not yet built.

Tab 4 split into two (2026-09-21, per Matt's direct "Split tab 4 into two separate tabs"): "Nitrogen and Tillage: No Single Best Rate" is now two tabs -- "Does Tillage Matter?" (new) and "Nitrogen: No Single Best Rate" (the same content as before, minus tillage, retitled). Tillage now gets its own stub paragraph: hold site/weather/nitrogen fixed and vary only tillage practice across the same five real implements already in the engine, with the actual point being that the effect only shows up when nitrogen is the limiting factor -- already-verified in this session's tillage work (measurable yield gain at a low N rate, ~no effect at a non-limiting one), not a new claim. Total tab count is now 7, sequence: Watch a Season Grow, What Changes the Outcome?, Does Crop Choice Matter?, Does Tillage Matter?, Nitrogen (No Single Best Rate), Rotations and Cover Crops, Design Your Own Investigation.

While splitting, also renamed every tab's internal content-div id from positional (`tab-1`...`tab-6`) to feature-based (`tab-watch-season`, `tab-outcome-compare`, `tab-crop-choice`, `tab-tillage`, `tab-nitrogen`, `tab-rotation`, `tab-open`) -- a deliberate, proactive application of the exact lesson already learned the hard way in the engineering unit (Tab 4/Tab 5 swap there needed no internal id changes specifically because those ids were already named for the feature, not the position). Doing this now, while the file is small and still under active review, is cheap; doing it after more tabs depend on stable ids would not be. Verified via Playwright: all 7 tabs render in the right order with the right headings, clicking each activates exactly one content div, and Tab 1's real iframe (unaffected by this restructuring) still loads correctly.

Tab names made parallel (2026-09-21, per Matt's direct "I don't like how some are yes/no questions, one gives the punchline (nitrogen), and they are not parallel" -- a fair, specific complaint, not a vague style preference): "Nitrogen: No Single Best Rate" was flagged separately as worth noticing on its own, since it's literally the exact "Phrase: longer phrase" title format this file's own "How Matt works" section says never to use, and it had leaked from chat-response style into an actual product tab name. All seven renamed to plain, parallel noun phrases naming the topic without stating the conclusion: Season Progress, Weather and Soil, Crop Choice, Tillage Practice, Nitrogen Rate, Rotations and Cover Crops, Open Investigation. Applied to both the nav button labels and each panel's own `<h2>` (which had silently drifted from the nav label anyway, carrying the same yes/no-question and punchline problems a second time) so the two can't read differently. Also fixed two smaller instances of the same yes/no pattern inside Tab 2's own body copy ("Does weather matter?" / "Does soil matter?" bolded lead-ins) to plain "Weather." / "Soil." labels, for full consistency rather than leaving the pattern intact one level down. Verified via Playwright that all 7 nav labels and their corresponding panel headings now match exactly.

Tab 2 ("Weather and Soil") built for real (2026-09-21, per Matt's "Let's start scoping tab 2" then "Go ahead and build it," with a real correction along the way): scoping surfaced a genuine problem with the pair implied by the original stub copy ("2012 dry vs. 1993 wet") -- checked directly against the real engine and found 2012 actually slightly OUTYIELDS 1993 at Rock Springs at every nitrogen rate tested (10.50 vs 9.83 Mg/ha at N=650, same direction at N=150 and N=50). 2012's real national drought reputation doesn't transfer to this specific site's own growing-season precipitation, which isn't even in the driest 10 of the 37-year record here -- a framing this project's own copy had repeated in several places without ever checking it against this site's actual numbers. Replaced with a real, verified pair: 2016 (this record's actual lowest-yielding year, 436mm growing-season precip) vs. 1996 (actual highest-yielding, 840mm), a genuine +54% gap at N=650, consistent in direction at every N rate checked. Separately, Matt pushed back on whether the predict step needed a magnitude guess (a numeric "predict the gap size") -- agreed after reconsidering: a magnitude guess has no clean way to grade "how close counts as right" and adds cognitive overhead this early in the sequence without a clear payoff; simplified to a binary pick (which of the two wins), the same proven, zero-ambiguity mechanism already used in meteorology's Predict & Compare and engineering's Push & Pull. Matt also asked whether N=150 or N=650 gave a more noticeable difference; confirmed both comparisons show a bigger gap at N=650 (weather: +54% vs +37% at N=150; soil: Iowa/Kansas gap grows from +108% to +137%, since Kansas turns out to already be at its water-limited ceiling at N=150 -- more nitrogen does nothing for Kansas specifically, only Iowa responds further) -- switched both to N=650.

Built as two new panels in `engine-demo.html` itself (`#panel-year-compare`, `#panel-soil-compare`), not a separate file, following Tab 1's exact architecture: two guess buttons, disabled until the engine loads, and disabled again after a guess is made; clicking a guess runs both sides via Pyodide and reveals a bar chart plus a correct/incorrect verdict naming the real winner and the real gap. "Compare two years" reuses `ALL_WX`/`CORN`/`build_season_rows` directly (fixed years 2016/1996, N=650, both module-level `YEAR_COMPARE_A/B/N` constants). "Compare two soils" reuses the field-comparison panel's own `resolveSiteAt()` (real STATSGO2 soil + real NLDAS-2 weather-tile lookup) against two fixed `PRESET_SITES` entries (Central Iowa, Western Kansas) rather than a KML upload or free site choice -- necessary for a predict-then-check exercise to have a stable, known-in-advance answer. A new `runResolvedSiteSeason(resolved, year, nRate)` generalizes `runFieldSlot()`'s call-scoped-`dict(CORN)` pattern to an explicit resolved site/year/rate instead of reading the field-comparison panel's own `fieldState`, so this new panel can never leak into (or be leaked into by) that panel's state regardless of click order -- the same cross-contamination discipline already established for the field-comparison panel itself.

The embed-mode mechanism from Tab 1 was generalized from a single hardcoded `run-season` check to an `EMBED_PANELS` lookup table mapping `?embed=` values to panel ids (`run-season`, `year-compare`, `soil-compare`), so adding a future embeddable panel means one more table entry, not another copy of the hide-everything-else logic. Both new embed values also hide "Select your site" like `run-season` does, since neither new panel depends on `currentSite` -- both run fixed, hardcoded comparisons by design, not whatever site a student happened to pick elsewhere on the page.

Verified in plain CPython against the real embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE`/`DEMO_GLUE_SOURCE`/weather-tiles/soil-grid (all real, already-committed files, not mocked): the year-compare Python body reproduces 2016=8.07/1996=12.45 Mg/ha through the embedded engine's own weather blob; a from-scratch Python replica of `resolveSiteAt()`'s real tile-decode and nearest-soil-cell logic (built specifically to check this before shipping, since the browser-side decoder had never been independently re-derived in Python before) resolved Central Iowa and Western Kansas at 2012/N=650 to 5.82 and 2.45 Mg/ha respectively, matching the soil-compare panel's own embedded Python body to within 0.001 Mg/ha. Verified the JS wiring in headless Chromium: both new `?embed=` values isolate exactly the right panel; a mocked-Pyodide run of "Compare two years" with a deliberately wrong guess shows the correct "Not quite" verdict, real winner, and real gap, with both buttons staying disabled after; "Compare two soils" was run with only `pyodide.runPythonAsync` mocked and `resolveSiteAt()` untouched, confirming a real ~865KB weather-tile fetch and decode actually happened (not assumed) and the real Canisteo/Manter soil series names appear correctly in the verdict text. A screenshot of the full Tab 2 page confirms both panels render cleanly with the established visual language.

`investigation.html`'s own Tab 2 copy was kept deliberately short -- one intro paragraph about the predict-first mechanism, then both iframes directly -- rather than also repeating each comparison's own framing a second time outside the iframe, since that text already lives inside each embedded panel itself.

Tab 3 ("Crop Choice") built for real (2026-09-21, same day, per Matt's "Yes, go ahead and build it"): scoping again turned up a real gap between the stub's claim and the actual numbers, same discipline as Tab 2. The stub said "soybean's yield is flat, corn's rises" as if corn simply wins once nitrogen is available -- checked directly and found something richer: at Rock Springs 2012, soybean actually OUTYIELDS corn at low nitrogen (4.36 vs 2.93 Mg/ha at N=0), and corn only overtakes it past a real, computed crossover point (~37.2 kg N/ha, found by linear interpolation between the two bracketing swept rates where the sign of corn-minus-soybean flips). Above that point corn keeps climbing to a real ceiling (10.50 Mg/ha by N~400) while soybean stays exactly flat at every rate checked (a legume, fixes its own nitrogen, so the knob genuinely does nothing to it either way). A fixed-nitrogen-rate comparison (what the stub had implied) would have shown only one side of this and risked implying corn simply "wins," missing the actual lesson: which crop is better depends on the nitrogen decision, the two aren't independent choices.

Built as a third new panel in `engine-demo.html` (`#panel-crop-compare`), following the same predict-then-reveal shape as Tab 2's two panels but scoped differently: the guess is only about the N=0 point (which crop wins with no fertilizer, a real binary question with a real answer), but the reveal is the full nitrogen sweep for both crops overlaid on one chart, not just the single guessed point -- so a correct OR incorrect guess both lead to the same fuller picture, the crossover itself, rather than the reveal being redundant with the guess. Required a new general-purpose `drawLineChart(canvas, xValues, series, opts)` function (multiple color-coded series against one real numeric x-axis, plus an optional dashed vertical marker with a label) since neither existing chart helper fit: `drawSeasonChart` is hardcoded to a 0-1 y-range and named day-index fields, `drawBarChart` draws discrete bars not connected lines. `findCrossover(rates, corn, soy)` does the actual interpolation, checked to return `null` gracefully rather than crash if the two curves never crossed across the full swept range (verified this doesn't happen with the real numbers here, not just assumed).

Verified in plain CPython against the real embedded engine and weather data: the exact new Python sweep body reproduces corn's full 14-point curve (2.927 -> 10.499 Mg/ha, plateauing by N=400) and soybean's flat 4.362 at every point, matching the earlier standalone-harness numbers exactly, and the crossover computes to 37.22 kg N/ha through the embedded engine specifically, not just the standalone reference. Verified the JS wiring in headless Chromium with a mocked `pyodide` (physics already covered by the CPython check): `?embed=crop-compare` isolates exactly the right panel, guessing "corn" (wrong at N=0) produces the correct "Not quite" verdict naming soybean as the real N=0 winner and stating the crossover value in plain language, and both buttons stay disabled after a guess. A screenshot with the real computed numbers confirms the chart itself renders correctly -- flat red soybean line, rising-then-plateauing blue corn line, a dashed marker at the real crossover point, labeled.

Tab 4 ("Tillage Practice") scoped and built for real (2026-09-21, same day, per "Let's start scoping tab 4" then "Yes, go ahead and build it"): scoping this time found a real, already-shipped bug rather than a wrong assumed narrative like Tabs 2 and 3. The shared `TILLAGE_OPTIONS` array (backing three already-live panels -- the field-comparison panel, the nitrogen-sweep panel, and the rotation panel) listed "Field cultivator (light, reduced-till)" before "Chisel plow (reduced-till)," implying cultivator is the gentler of the two. Checked directly against `TILLAGE_IMPLEMENTS`' real `mixing_efficiency` values: chisel plow is 0.265919, field cultivator is 0.371806 -- the reverse of what the label and ordering claimed, confirmed further by actual yield differences at every nitrogen rate tested. Fixed by reordering `TILLAGE_OPTIONS` to the real intensity order (none, chisel, cultivator, disk, moldboard) and removing the wrong "light" claim, replacing it with "moderate" on the cultivator and "moderate-heavy" on disk -- a single shared array, so the fix propagates automatically to all three existing consumers with no separate edits needed (confirmed, not assumed -- see verification below).

Built a fourth new panel, `#panel-tillage-compare`, embedded into `investigation.html`'s Tab 4 via `?embed=tillage-compare` (a new `EMBED_PANELS` entry), following the same predict-then-reveal shape as Tabs 2-3's panels: predict whether the five tillage practices' yield spread is bigger at a low, nitrogen-limiting rate (50 kg N/ha) or a high, non-limiting one (650 kg N/ha), both at Rock Springs corn, 2012 -- then reveal two bar charts (reusing the existing `drawBarChart` helper, no new chart code needed) showing all five implements at both rates. Real numbers, freshly verified rather than trusted from memory: at N=50, grain ranges 4.854 (no tillage) to 5.275 Mg/ha (moldboard plow), an 8.7% spread; at N=650, all five implements converge to exactly 10.4987 Mg/ha, a real, already-established result (tillage's soil-mixing and background-N boost only reach the plant when nitrogen is actually limiting growth) now made visible directly rather than asserted in stub copy.

Verified four ways: (1) a from-scratch CPython check across all five implements at N=50/150/650 reproduced the previously-documented numbers to four decimals (no tillage 4.8542/7.8606/10.4987, chisel 4.9957/7.9571/10.4987, cultivator 5.0516/7.9950/10.4987, disk 5.2010/8.0960/10.4987, moldboard 5.2746/8.1455/10.4987); (2) the exact literal Python string used inside `runTillageCompareSet()` (not a reimplementation) was extracted verbatim and executed in plain CPython against the real embedded engine, reproducing the same N=50 and N=650 arrays exactly; (3) headless Chromium with a mocked `pyodide` confirmed `?embed=tillage-compare` isolates exactly the right panel, the corrected dropdown order/labels now render in all three pre-existing consuming panels (field-comparison, nitrogen-sweep, rotation) as well as the new one, guessing correctly produces the right verdict text with both real spread percentages, and both buttons stay disabled after a guess; (4) a screenshot with the real computed numbers confirms both bar charts render correctly -- a visible rising staircase across the five practices at N=50, uniformly flat bars at N=650. Also verified in headless Chromium that `investigation.html`'s Tab 4 nav/heading show "Tillage Practice" with no stub tag, the iframe resolves to the correct embed URL, and the inner embedded page correctly isolates just the tillage-compare panel with its header hidden.

Tab 5 ("Nitrogen Rate") scoped and rebuilt from a different plan than the stub described (2026-09-21, same day): the stub's plan was to reuse the already-built "How much does nitrogen matter?" panel (nitrogen sweep, economic overlay, three-perspectives frontier chart) largely as-is. Scoping that plan directly turned up two real problems before any decision was Matt's to make. First, the predict-then-reveal shape used for Tabs 2-4 doesn't fit every tab, and Matt said so directly rather than let it get shoehorned in here -- noted and dropped as a hard requirement going forward, not just for this tab. Second, and more load-bearing: the existing panel's own copy says it "runs the selected crop and year (from 'Run a season' above)" -- crop and year live in a different panel's DOM entirely, so embedding it alone via `?embed=` (which hides every other panel) would have stranded a student with no way to pick either. That gap wasn't going away regardless of the predict-then-reveal question.

Matt then redirected the tab's actual content: not the stakeholder-perspective framing (farmer/water-quality advocate/policymaker, already correctly slated for Tab 7's stub, not duplicated here), but the nitrogen cycle itself -- applied nitrogen not taken up by the crop has to go somewhere, and where depends on rain, soil, and topography. Checked that claim directly against the engine before building anything: `runoff_mm()` (a real curve-number function with a real slope term, Eq. SI.1-7) exists in `cycles_engine_validate.py` but is never called anywhere in `simulate_season()` -- confirmed by grepping for call sites and finding only the definition. The actual water balance (`redistribute(layers, w["pp"] + irrigation_mm)`) sends all precipitation straight to infiltration with zero runoff subtraction. So today: soil texture genuinely affects nitrogen fate (already validated, already shown in Tab 2's Iowa/Kansas comparison), rain genuinely affects it (already shown in Tab 2's year comparison), but topography does nothing at all -- not a simplification, dead code, and there's no real slope data anywhere in this project's datasets even if it were wired in. Flagged this rather than build a tab implying a mechanism that doesn't exist; Matt's call was to stay leaching-only for this tab and, since Cycles' own published runoff equation is the thing not wired in, treat that as the actual disclosed gap rather than inventing an undisclosed slope effect to compensate.

Engine change, additive, in both `cycles_engine_validate.py` and the embedded copy in `engine-demo.html`: `simulate_season()` now tracks `n_uptake_total` (cumulative nitrogen actually drawn into the crop) alongside the existing `n_leached_total`, and returns both `n_uptake_kg_ha` and `n_remaining_kg_ha` (whatever's left in the mineral-N pool at season end) whenever nitrogen tracking is active. Together with the already-existing `n_leached_kg_ha` these three give a complete mass balance. Verified directly for Rock Springs 2012 corn at four rates that supply exactly equals uptake + leached + remaining every time (N=0: sum 65.0 = ~65 kg background mineralization over the season; N=50: sum 115.0; N=150: sum 215.0; N=650: sum 715.0 -- each exactly rate + background, no leaks). Re-ran `run_validation.py`/`run_validation_rotation2.py` unchanged and got the exact previously-documented correlations (corn 0.540 against currently-available reference data, soybean 0.846, wheat 0.253, silage corn 0.501) confirming this is purely additive.

Built as a new panel, `#panel-n-balance` ("Nitrogen balance"), embedded via a new `?embed=n-balance` `EMBED_PANELS` entry -- a live exploration tool, not predict-then-reveal, per Matt's direction. Self-contained (no dependency on "Run a season"'s crop/year controls, sidestepping the embedding problem found above): one nitrogen-rate slider (0-650, default 150) driving two fixed comparisons reusing already-validated real data -- a dry year vs. a wet one at Rock Springs corn (2016/1996, the same real pair Tab 2's year-compare panel uses) and a sandy soil vs. a clay-rich one (Central Iowa/Western Kansas via `resolveSiteAt()`, the same real sites and 2012 year Tab 2's soil-compare panel uses). A "Run comparison" button fires four `simulate_season()` calls (reusing the existing `runNamedSeason()`/`runResolvedSiteSeason()` helpers unmodified) and draws two new three-segment stacked bar charts (`drawNBalanceBars()`, a new small canvas helper: taken up / leached / remaining, each bar summing to that scenario's real total N supply) side by side per comparison. Real numbers checked before writing any UI, via a from-scratch Python replica of `resolveSiteAt()`'s tile-decode and nearest-soil-cell logic built specifically for this (the browser-side decoder had been re-derived in Python once before for Tab 2 but not saved, so it was rebuilt): at N=150, Rock Springs 2016/1996 split 199/13/0 vs 201/21/0 (uptake/leached/remaining, kg N/ha); Iowa/Kansas at 2012 split 205/2/0 vs 190/0/6 -- Kansas's dry, low-drainage soil doesn't leach the surplus out at all, it just sits in the pool, a genuinely different failure mode from Iowa's wetter profile flushing it downstream. At a much higher rate (N=550) the story sharpens considerably: Kansas leaves 400 kg N/ha sitting unused in the soil pool, Iowa 282, and the rain comparison's leaching gap widens from 8 to 53 kg N/ha -- confirms the panel is worth having a slider for, not just a fixed illustrative rate.

Verified two ways, the same split this file always uses for anything Pyodide-dependent: (1) the real embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE`/`DEMO_GLUE_SOURCE`/weather-tiles/soil-grid, all real committed files, driving the from-scratch Python resolver above to reproduce the four real scenario splits quoted above, both at N=150 and N=550, and independently confirming the mass-balance identity holds at every one of them. (2) Headless Chromium with only `pyodide.runPythonAsync`/`globals.set` mocked and `resolveSiteAt()` left untouched (so the real ~865KB weather-tile fetch and real soil-grid fetch both actually happen, not assumed): `?embed=n-balance` isolates exactly the right panel; moving the slider updates its live label; clicking "Run comparison" fires the rain comparison first, then resolves real Iowa/Kansas soil+weather, then fires the soil comparison, then shows the result block; both stacked-bar canvases render non-blank pixels. Also verified in headless Chromium that `investigation.html`'s Tab 5 nav/heading read "Nitrogen Rate" with no stub tag, the iframe resolves to `?embed=n-balance`, and the inner embedded page correctly isolates just the nitrogen-balance panel with its header hidden. A full-page screenshot confirms both stacked bars render legibly with correct labels, legend, and totals.

Tab 6 ("Rotations and Cover Crops") scoped and built (2026-09-21, same day): unlike every tab scoped before it this session, the stub's headline claim held up rather than needing correction. Verified directly against the already-built "Simulate a rotation" panel across 8 real year/nitrogen combinations (2012/1996/2015/1993 as starting years, corn N from 0-650, cover-crop N from 0-100): the cover crop leaches less nitrogen than bare fallow every single time, from a 3.3 kg N/ha gap up to an 11.1 kg N/ha gap depending on year and rate -- robust, not a cherry-picked example. One real overstatement did need fixing: the stub claimed this tab "combines everything from the earlier tabs (crop choice, nitrogen, tillage)," but the rotation sequence itself (corn -> wheat cover -> soybean) is fixed, not a live choice -- there's no actual crop-choice control here, unlike nitrogen and tillage, which both are. Fixed by dropping "crop choice" from the tab's copy rather than building a feature the stub merely implied.

Also confirmed while checking those 8 combinations: this panel's own already-disclosed "cheap version" limitation (each phase runs independently, no real soil-state carryover) is real and consistent -- the cover crop's own leaching/fallow numbers came out byte-identical whether corn nitrogen was 150 or 650, exactly as expected since "nitrogen available to the cover crop" is a slider input, not something the corn phase's leftover nitrogen feeds into.

Built as a straight embed of the already-existing panel, no predict-then-reveal and no new mechanic, per Matt's own "I don't think we need to add [more]" -- matching Tab 1's precedent of reusing an already-built, already-validated panel wholesale rather than every tab needing a bespoke build. Added `id="panel-rotation"` to the existing panel (it previously had none) and a new `?embed=rotation` `EMBED_PANELS` entry; the panel was already fully self-contained (its own year/tillage/nitrogen-rate controls, no dependency on "Run a season" or `currentSite`), so unlike Tab 5 this needed no embedding-architecture fix at all.

Verified in headless Chromium: `?embed=rotation` isolates exactly the right panel (header, "Select your site," and every other panel hidden); `investigation.html`'s Tab 6 nav/heading read "Rotations and Cover Crops" with no stub tag and no "crop choice" text anywhere in the tab's copy; the iframe resolves to `?embed=rotation`; the inner embedded page correctly isolates just the rotation panel with its own year select, tillage select, both nitrogen sliders, and "Run rotation" button all present. A full-page screenshot confirms the embedded panel renders cleanly with the established visual language.

Deliberately not built, per Matt directly ("I don't think we need to add N-fate breakdown... but maybe put it in CLAUDE.md in case we want to return to it"): the rotation panel's own Pyodide calls already return `n_uptake_kg_ha`/`n_remaining_kg_ha` for both the corn and cover-crop phases (Tab 5's mass-balance fields flow through automatically, no extra plumbing needed), but the JS only extracts `n_leached` today. A real, cheap extension if this is ever revisited: reuse Tab 5's `drawNBalanceBars()` to show the same taken-up/leached/remaining breakdown for the cover-crop phase here, which would tie the two tabs together -- checked in passing during this scoping pass that the numbers are real and worth showing (e.g. at cover N=100, 37.7 kg N/ha sits unused in the pool rather than leaching, the same "excess doesn't just vanish" pattern Tab 5 teaches, showing up again in a more realistic multi-phase scenario). Not built now because it wasn't asked for, not because the data or the helper function are missing.

Tab 7 ("Open Investigation") scoped and built (2026-09-21, same day): the biggest single build in this unit's guided investigation so far, and the only tab this session where the stub's plan itself changed materially before any code got written.

Scoping found the stub's ambition (every condition -- site, weather year, crop, nitrogen rate and timing, tillage, rotation -- becomes a live choice, plus a run-by-run log) was a much larger build than the tabs before it, most of which were embed-or-lightly-fix jobs. Checked what's actually wired up: site, crop (corn/soybean), year, nitrogen rate, and tillage all already work as live conditions somewhere in this file; nitrogen timing (`n_applications`, a real engine parameter since the six-mechanisms session) and rotation-as-a-choice (`SPECIES_REGISTRY`, deliberately left unconnected to any picker) have zero UI anywhere; a run-by-run log doesn't exist in this unit at all. Recommended shipping the five already-working conditions plus a log and a claim/feedback step, flagging the rest as a real follow-up rather than building everything at once.

Matt redirected further: rather than exposing every condition as a live student choice, autogenerate a scenario -- a randomly-assigned real field -- and have the student design policy around the three-part tension (farmers need profit, the environment matters, sustainable solutions are genuinely hard), with AI feedback reinforcing evidence-based argument. Checked this computationally before building anything, since naive full-CONUS randomization has a real failure mode: sampled 40 real sites (real STATSGO2 soil + real NLDAS-2 weather, not synthetic) and ran the exact farmer/water-advocate/policymaker computation already built for Tab 5 at each, fixed at 2012 corn and default prices. Result: 8 of 39 resolved sites (~20%) landed on arid/mountain cells (Lopez CA, Pico CA, Cleaver NV, Gabbvally NV, Ninemile OR, Kydestea AZ, Calvista AZ, Tobosa TX) where rainfed corn barely grows and all three perspectives collapse to the identical rate -- no tension, nothing to argue about. The farmer's rate differed from the water advocate's rate in 31/39 (~79%) of samples, a solid base rate for a real fix rather than a coin flip. This is the same problem `anthropology/assessment.html` already solved for mystery specimens (its own docstring: naive independent-field randomization "would risk generating a specimen that trivially resolves on one metric") -- same category of fix applies here.

Built, reusing as much already-verified machinery as possible rather than a from-scratch build: a new panel, `#panel-open-investigation` ("Set a nitrogen policy for your assigned field"), embedded via a new `?embed=open-investigation` `EMBED_PANELS` entry.

- **Seeded randomization**: `mulberry32()` and the seed-lives-in-`?seed=`-URL pattern are copied verbatim from `anthropology/assessment.html` (cross-checked byte-for-byte against real Node output at four different seeds to 10 decimal places before trusting the port) -- a reload keeps the same field, a fresh visit assigns a new one, for the same anti-sharing reason assessment.html already established.
- **Reject-and-resample scenario generation** (`oiGenerateScenario()`): draws a random real cell from the same committed `weather-tiles/` manifest already used elsewhere on this page (not a synthetic point), resolves it via the existing `resolveSiteAt()`, runs a real 14-point nitrogen sweep, and accepts the first draw where the farmer's and water advocate's rates genuinely differ -- `OI_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 8`, chosen since the measured ~79% single-draw success rate makes an 8-attempt failure a roughly 1-in-4-million event. Falls back to the largest-gap attempt seen if every one somehow comes back degenerate, rather than looping forever.
- **`computeThreePerspectives()`**: a new pure function, deliberately a duplicate of `renderProfitChart()`'s own farmer/water-advocate/policymaker logic (Tab 5's already-shipped panel) rather than a refactor of it -- avoids any regression risk to Tab 5's already-verified behavior while this new panel was being built.
- **The frontier chart itself needed zero new charting code**: `drawFrontierChart()` was already fully generic (`points` + a `highlights` array) from Tab 5's own build, so this panel just calls it with a fourth highlight, "Your policy," recomputed live as a nitrogen-rate slider (snapped to the same 50 kg N/ha steps as the sweep, so it always lands on a real computed point, no interpolation needed) moves.
- **Claim/evidence/tradeoffs/reflection + AI feedback**: this unit's first use of the shared `FEEDBACK_ENDPOINT` Cloudflare Worker (anthropology Tab 5, engineering Tab 7 are the other two consumers) -- no worker changes needed, same generic `{systemPrompt, userPrompt}` -> `{text}` contract, copied faithfully from engineering Tab 7's exact request/response/error-handling shape. The new system prompt hands the model the student's actual run numbers (their chosen rate's yield/leaching/profit, plus all three reference perspectives) as the only ground truth, and is told to push back if the argument doesn't cite those numbers, doesn't name which perspective it prioritizes, claims the choice is costless to the other two, or treats one run as proof.

Verified four ways: (1) the exact literal `mulberry32()` JS function run in real Node produced identical output to 10 decimal places against an independent Python port at four different seeds, before that Python port was trusted for anything else. (2) The exact literal Python snippet from `oiRunSweep()` and a Python port of the full reject-resample loop, run against the real committed `weather-tiles/`/`statsgo2_soil_grid.json.gz` data, found a non-degenerate scenario in 1-2 attempts (well under the 8-attempt cap) across 10 different real seeds, and confirmed the same seed run twice returns the exact same field (real determinism, not assumed). (3) Headless Chromium with only `pyodide.runPythonAsync`/`globals.set` mocked and `resolveSiteAt()` left untouched: fed the mock a deliberately degenerate first sweep followed by a genuinely spread second one, confirming the reject-resample loop actually retries (2 real Pyodide calls, not 1) rather than accepting the first bad draw; confirmed the rendered field info, table, and non-blank frontier chart canvas; confirmed the nitrogen-rate slider updates its label and the chart's "Your policy" marker live with the correct readout text; confirmed the claim form blocks on empty fields with an alert, displays a mocked successful response, and falls back to the same graceful "try again" text as the other two `FEEDBACK_ENDPOINT` consumers on a mocked network failure; confirmed the seed persists across a reload with an explicit `?seed=` and that a fresh visit with no seed auto-generates and writes one to the URL. (4) `investigation.html`'s Tab 7 nav/heading read "Open Investigation" with no stub tag, the iframe resolves to `?embed=open-investigation`, and the inner embedded page correctly isolates just this panel with its header hidden. A full-page screenshot of the standalone panel and of the embedded tab both confirm clean rendering. The actual live Pyodide load-and-run (real init() never completes in this sandbox, the standing network-egress limitation documented throughout this file) still needs a real-browser check before trusting it in front of students, same caveat as every other Pyodide-dependent feature here.

Not built, deliberately: nitrogen timing, rotation-as-a-choice, irrigation, and manure stay off this tab's live-condition list, exactly as scoped -- real engine capabilities, but adding their UI is a separate, larger effort flagged for a later round, not bundled into this one. No run-by-run log exists yet either; this tab is currently a single assigned-scenario-per-visit exercise, not a multi-run comparison tool. This completes the originally-planned 7-tab structure for the agricultural unit's guided investigation -- every tab now has real content, no stubs left, matching the milestone already reached by the anthropology and meteorology units' own guided investigations.

Real live-browser bug fixed, first genuine post-merge finding (2026-09-21, same day, after Matt's first real runthrough): Matt reported all five tillage-practice bars reading identical -- a real bug, not a modeling gap, and one this session's whole CPython-verification discipline structurally could not have caught, since CPython never exercises Pyodide's actual concurrent scheduling. Root cause: three call sites (the tillage-compare panel's low/high nitrogen comparison, and the nitrogen-balance panel's rain and soil comparisons) fired two `pyodide.runPythonAsync()` calls concurrently via `Promise.all()`, but both calls write their inputs into the *same shared Pyodide global namespace* (`tc_n_rate` etc.) before either one's Python body actually reads it back. Real Pyodide yields to the JS event loop internally (loading imports) before running the code string, so the second call's `globals.set()` fires and overwrites the first call's inputs before the first call's Python ever executes -- both runs silently ended up using whichever rate won the race (in JS's left-to-right evaluation order, almost certainly the second, higher one). At 650 kg N/ha all five tillage implements are already validated to converge to the identical value, which is exactly why every bar looked the same rather than showing the expected staircase at the low rate. Two already-shipped panels (Tab 2's year-compare and soil-compare) do the equivalent comparison correctly, sequentially with `await`, not `Promise.all` -- confirmed as the reason Matt saw this only on tillage, not there. Fixed by serializing all three call sites to sequential `await`s, the same pattern the working panels already used; verified with a mocked-Pyodide test that reproduces Pyodide's real yield-before-execute timing (a `setTimeout` delay inside the mocked `runPythonAsync`, deliberately built to actually expose the race rather than assume it away) and confirms each call now reads back the rate it was actually given, both at call time and at "execution" time. Worth remembering the shape of this bug for any future panel in this project that runs two `pyodide.runPythonAsync()` calls side by side: Pyodide has exactly one global Python namespace, so concurrent calls that both use `pyodide.globals.set()` are unsafe by construction, regardless of how independent the two calls look in JS -- `Promise.all()` is only safe here when every promise in it is pure JS (like `resolveSiteAt()`'s fetch/decode work), never when any of them touch `pyodide.globals`.

Tab 7 rebuilt from a randomly-assigned scenario into a real student-driven investigation (2026-09-21, same day, per Matt's direct feedback after his first runthrough: "the open investigation seems more like the final test, not a chance to take CYCLES 'out for a spin'"). Stress-tested his own proposal before building (per this file's own "lead with what's wrong, then recommend" discipline): flagged that "change some levers systematically" needed real structure or students would either flail or never vary anything, and that not every real engine lever has UI yet (nitrogen timing and rotation-as-a-choice are real `simulate_season()` parameters with zero UI anywhere). Matt confirmed scope: 4 already-live levers (crop, nitrogen rate, tillage, weather year) plus a run log modeled on the engineering unit's own Iteration Log, with the KML tutorial placed in Tab 2 rather than Tab 7 itself.

What changed: the old auto-generated-scenario machinery (`mulberry32`, seed-in-URL, `oiGenerateScenario`'s reject-resample loop, `oiAllCandidateCells`) is gone entirely, not just hidden -- a student now uploads their own real field via KML (reusing `parseFieldKML`/`fieldPolygonCentroid`/`resolveSiteAt`, the exact same pipeline the field-comparison panel and "Select your site" already use), then runs the model repeatedly under crop/nitrogen-rate/tillage/year combinations they choose, each run appended to a growing log table (never overwritten). `runOiScenario()` generalizes `runFieldSlot()`'s exact call-scoped-`dict(crop)` pattern from a fixed `CORN`/150 to a variable crop (via the `CROPS` registry) and N rate. Once at least 2 runs are logged, a synthesis section unlocks: a "See reference perspectives" button runs a 14-point N sweep at the student's current crop/tillage/year (a single Pyodide call, not two -- deliberately not repeating the `Promise.all` race just fixed above) and reuses `drawFrontierChart()` with zero new charting code, but shows only the farmer (max profit) and water-quality advocate (min leaching within an 80% yield floor) reference points, not a third auto-computed "policymaker" dot -- per Matt's explicit "only the final assessment need be the policymaker," the student's own written recommendation is the sole policymaker position, not one of three computed answers to match. Soybean is gated out of the reference sweep (nitrogen doesn't affect it, so there's no real tradeoff to show) with a plain explanatory message rather than a wasted Pyodide call, verified to fire zero sweep calls. The claim/evidence/tradeoffs/reflection form and its `FEEDBACK_ENDPOINT` system prompt were rewritten to hand the model the student's actual multi-run log as ground truth (not a single scenario's three reference numbers), explicitly told to push back if the argument doesn't cite specific logged runs, doesn't explain what isolating one lever at a time showed, or never actually reasons as a policymaker weighing a real cost.

A real, foreseeable failure mode was caught and fixed before it could bite a real student: Google Earth's own "Export as KML file" option (confirmed via web search against Google's own developer docs, since this sandbox's egress policy blocks fetching google.com directly) can still produce a `.kmz` file despite its name -- KMZ is a zipped container this tool's `parseFieldKML()` has never been able to read (it only ever expected plain XML text). Rather than let that surface as an opaque "not valid KML/XML" parser error, `parseFieldKML()` now checks for the ZIP magic bytes ("PK", which survive UTF-8 decoding intact even though the rest of a binary zip won't) and throws a specific, actionable message pointing back to the tutorial -- verified with a fake `"PK\x03\x04..."` upload producing exactly that message instead of the generic one.

Tutorial added to Tab 2 ("Weather and Soil"), per Matt's direct placement choice: a new panel with numbered steps (Google Earth Pro recommended, with its explicit "Save as type: Kml" dropdown; Google Earth Web as an alternative, with the .kmz caveat above stated plainly) followed by a live practice embed of the already-built "Select your site" panel (`?embed=select-site`, a new `EMBED_PANELS` entry) so a student can upload a real KML and see real STATSGO2/NLDAS-2 data resolve before Tab 7 requires the same skill for real. Explicitly stated as practice-only in the copy -- it does not carry forward to Tab 7, which has its own independent upload step; unifying the two was considered and deliberately not done, since Tab 7's upload is the one that actually matters and keeping it self-contained means the tab works even if a student skips or forgets Tab 2's practice run.

Verified two ways, the same split this file always uses for anything Pyodide-dependent: (1) the exact literal `runOiScenario()`/reference-sweep Python snippets extracted verbatim and run in plain CPython against real Central Iowa STATSGO2 soil + real NLDAS-2 weather (via a from-scratch Python port of `resolveSiteAt()` already built earlier this session) -- confirmed physically sensible, non-trivial results: corn N=150/no-tillage/2012 gives 5.105 Mg/ha (matching this file's own earlier-documented 5.11 Mg/ha for the same site/year), moldboard tillage measurably raises that to 5.251, 1993 (wetter) gives higher yield and much higher leaching than 2012 (7.968 Mg/ha vs 35.4 kg N/ha leached, vs 2012's 1.875), soybean runs cleanly with no nitrogen fields at all, and the reference sweep produces a genuine farmer/water-advocate split (N=250 vs N=150) with soybean correctly flat throughout. (2) Headless Chromium with only `pyodide.globals.set`/`runPythonAsync` mocked and `resolveSiteAt()` left untouched (a real KML upload triggers a real fetch against the real committed `weather-tiles/`/`statsgo2_soil_grid.json.gz`, not assumed): confirmed the new `?embed=select-site` value isolates the right panel without breaking any existing embed value (`run-season` still correctly hides "Select your site," the one regression risk in this change); confirmed a real KML point resolves to the real Canisteo/Iowa series; confirmed exactly one Pyodide call fires per logged run (two clicks, two calls, not four -- no repeat of the concurrency bug fixed earlier the same day); confirmed the run log appends rather than overwrites and the synthesis section unlocks exactly at 2 logged runs, not 1; confirmed the reference-perspectives button fires one real sweep call for corn and zero for soybean; confirmed the claim form's empty-field alert, mocked-success, and mocked-failure paths all still work. A full-page screenshot of the rebuilt panel and of `investigation.html`'s Tab 2 (tutorial + practice embed) both confirm clean rendering matching the established visual language. The actual live Pyodide load-and-run in a real browser still needs checking before trusting it in front of students, the same standing caveat as every Pyodide-dependent feature in this file.

Pedagogical review session and 8-item checklist (2026-09-22): Matt did a real tab-by-tab critique of the guided investigation after his first runthrough, working from the stated science-education goal (engage students in the actual practices scientists use a model for, which is rare in schools and the whole reason this unit is worth getting right, not just "run the numbers"). Real, specific findings, not general dissatisfaction: Tab 1's takeaway was thin ("weather affects yield" resolves to either fatalism or "just irrigate," no real so-what); Tab 2's two panels taught the same lesson twice with different variables; the predict-then-reveal binary-guess mechanic used in Tabs 2-4 isn't actual model-based inquiry, a click carries no reasoning, nothing to be wrong about except a number; Tab 3 stopped at "soybean is nitrogen-independent" without the real tradeoff (a lower yield ceiling) or the connection to rotations; Tab 4's tillage bars showed only minor differences with no stated reason, risking the wrong lesson ("no advantage, so why does anyone till") and had no Y-axis label at all; Tab 5's takeaway was never stated in words; Tab 6 showed an environmental benefit with no reason a farmer would care. Logged as 8 tracked tasks (TaskCreate #1-8): rebuild Tab 1 around real risk, differentiate Tab 2's two panels and add a reasoning field before each reveal, extend Tab 3's legume story, remove Tab 4 entirely (Matt's call: "if that's all we can show about tillage... there's nothing here of interest" -- not fixed, cut), state Tab 5's takeaway explicitly, wire a real nitrogen-credit mechanism into Tab 6, add Y-axis labels everywhere `drawBarChart` is used, and separately scope expanding crop/species choices (accuracy against real Cycles explicitly ruled out as a gating concern by Matt directly: "I don't care about the inaccuracy... I've been very clear about this repeatedly").

Tab 1 rebuilt around real climate risk and crop-choice adaptation (2026-09-22, same day, task #1): Matt's own "so what" concern sharpened the redesign -- his first fix idea (read the water-stress trace's timing within a season) still risked landing as fatalism, so the actual build instead runs the SAME choice (a site + corn hybrid) across the full 37-year weather record and shows the real distribution of outcomes, with the explicit takeaway "you can't control which year you get, but you do control which hybrid and which place you start from, and the rest of this investigation is about those controllable decisions." Matt then redirected further mid-build: he wants the "adaptation" idea (a crop well suited to a region because of climate, not year-to-year variance) to live here too, and said accuracy against real Cycles output is not a blocker for adding species right now.

Two real dead ends found and reported before building anything, exactly the kind of computational check this file has used throughout rather than assuming a plausible-sounding idea will work:
- **Sorghum vs. corn** (real GenericCrops.crop SorghumMS values ported and tested against Iowa vs. Kansas): showed sorghum uniformly ~26% ahead of corn at BOTH sites, no crossover at all -- the wrong lesson ("always plant sorghum"), not the intended one. Root cause, confirmed by reading this engine's actual water-stress mechanism rather than assuming real-world sorghum lore would transfer: this engine models species-level water response as a single flat water-use-efficiency multiplier with no stress-dependent behavior, so a real efficiency edge just scales up everywhere equally instead of only mattering where water is actually short. Real sorghum's rooting depth (2m) turned out to be identical to real corn's own value in the same crop file, and this engine doesn't even read rooting depth per-crop anyway (it's a `simulate_season()` call parameter, shared across all crops, not a crop-dict field) -- so neither of sorghum's two textbook drought-tolerance mechanisms was ever going to show up here.
- **Winter wheat vs. corn, at a genuinely cold site**: built the real engineering piece this needed first (wheat has no standalone "run to real harvest" path in this engine, only a truncated fall-to-spring cover-crop role terminated at DOY 100 -- reused `build_cover_crop_rows()`'s exact row-spanning logic with a much later `term_doy`, given `simulate_season()` already stops correctly on its own thermal-time-based harvest trigger once enough rows are supplied, so this needed zero new engine code, just a longer row window). Result still didn't show a cold-tolerance crossover: at North Dakota, wheat's relative yield penalty (moving from Iowa) came out LARGER than corn's, not smaller, the opposite of the intended lesson. Diagnosed properly, not just abandoned: a direct precipitation check showed North Dakota is genuinely drier than Iowa across both crops' windows, but the deficit compounds far more over wheat's much longer real fall-to-summer exposure window than over corn's shorter summer one -- confirmed by re-testing at a second, deliberately less-confounded site (north-central Minnesota, genuinely cold but not dramatically drier than Iowa, unlike the semi-arid Dakotas) and getting the same direction of result (wheat's relative penalty still larger than corn's), ruling out "just the wrong site" as the explanation. The wheat-to-harvest code itself works correctly (produces plausible 2.8-4.0 Mg/ha yields) and is left in place should it be useful later, but was not used for Tab 1.

What actually worked, verified before committing to build it: two corn hybrids differing ONLY in thermal-time-to-maturity (`CORN` = real GenericCrops.crop CornRM.90 exactly, already the validated corn entry; new `CORN_LONG_SEASON` = real CornRM.110 values, same rue/wue/temperature response/calibration, tt_maturity 2300 vs 1800, flowering_tt 1300 vs 1000). Tested at Iowa and North Dakota across the full 37-year record: at Iowa the long-season hybrid averages 8.71 Mg/ha vs. the short-season hybrid's 7.40 (17.7% ahead, more time to cash in the higher maturity requirement); at North Dakota the SHORT-season hybrid actually wins on the full-record average, 6.04 vs. 5.91 -- a genuine crossover, not an artifact of cherry-picked years, and directly analogous to real current agricultural practice (farmers further north plant lower relative-maturity hybrids specifically because a longer-season one risks not finishing before the season ends). This is real, isolated, and cheap: only one parameter differs between the two hybrids, and both use the same spring soil-temperature planting trigger as the existing validated corn, so no new planting-logic engineering was needed the way wheat would have required.

Site list extended: added a 5th preset, north-central North Dakota (48.23, -101.30, real Barnes series, a genuine glacial-till prairie soil, confirmed in-coverage), to the shared `PRESET_SITES` array used by every site-picking panel on this page (the "Select your site" panel, Field B, the field-comparison panel), not a separate list scoped to just this new panel -- chosen deliberately since it's a real, useful climate-diversity addition for any consumer of that array, and nothing in the codebase assumes a fixed length or indexes into it positionally.

Built as a new panel, `#panel-season-risk` ("Weather risk and crop choice"), a new `?embed=season-risk` `EMBED_PANELS` entry, replacing the "Run a season" single-day-by-day-trace panel as Tab 1's embedded content (that panel itself is untouched and still live in the standalone sandbox and reachable via its own existing `?embed=run-season`, just no longer what Tab 1 shows). Two independent (site, hybrid) choice slots, each a plain `<select>` pair (not a button row, a deliberate smaller variation from this file's usual site-picker pattern, chosen for compactness in a 2-column layout) defaulting to Central Iowa/short-season vs. North Dakota/long-season -- the single most illustrative pairing found. One "Run both across all 37 years" button fires two sequential (not `Promise.all`) 37-year sweeps, one Pyodide call per slot each looping 37 years server-side in Python (not 74 separate JS-to-Python calls), rendering two `drawBarChart` histograms plus a plain-language comparison note naming which choice has the higher 37-year average and which swings less year to year, explicitly stating that neither dimension is under the student's control except through the hybrid/site choice itself. Fixed at 150 kg N/ha for both slots, stated in the copy, so nitrogen isn't a confound (it gets its own tab later).

`drawBarChart()` gained an optional `yLabel` parameter while this was being built (closes checklist item #7 for every remaining consumer of that function): drawn as a small caption above the plot area rather than a true rotated axis label, with a small fixed top-margin reserved unconditionally so no existing call site's layout shifts depending on whether it opts in. Applied to every real consumer left after Tab 4's removal: the new season-risk panel, year-compare, soil-compare, the field-comparison panel's three charts, the rotation panel's leaching comparison, and the standalone year-sweep panel (not itself part of the guided investigation, but the same bug, fixed for consistency). `crop-compare` uses a different function (`drawLineChart`, built for Tab 3) and was left alone here, to be addressed together with Tab 3's own rebuild (checklist item #3), not scope-crept into this pass.

Verified the same two ways this file always uses for anything Pyodide-dependent: (1) the exact literal `runSeasonRiskSweep()` Python snippet extracted verbatim from the shipped HTML and run in plain CPython against real Iowa and North Dakota STATSGO2 soil + real NLDAS-2 weather -- reproduced the exact same per-year numbers already spot-checked by hand for both crops at both sites, and the full 37-year run confirmed the crossover holds on the complete record, not just the handful of years originally sampled. (2) Headless Chromium with only `pyodide.globals.set`/`runPythonAsync` mocked: confirmed `?embed=season-risk` isolates the right panel without breaking the existing `run-season` embed value (a real regression risk, since both now coexist as separate embeddable panels); confirmed the site/hybrid selects populate from the real (now 5-entry) `PRESET_SITES` with the correct Iowa/short-season and North-Dakota/long-season defaults; confirmed exactly 2 Pyodide calls fire per click (not a repeat of the earlier `Promise.all` concurrency bug -- these are sequential `await`s); confirmed both charts render non-blank with the Y-axis label present, and the comparison note correctly identifies the higher-average and more-reliable choice from whatever data it's given. One real test-harness mistake caught and fixed mid-session, not a product bug: the first pass at this exact test used a fixed 300ms wait after the click and read back an empty, not-yet-populated result, since two real `resolveSiteAt()` fetches (weather tile + soil grid, sequentially, one per slot) take longer than that -- switched to polling for `#srResult`'s visibility instead of guessing a delay, the same fixed-timeout trap this file has already documented once before (2026-09-17, the four-preset-sites round) and evidently worth restating since it recurred. A full-page screenshot of the standalone panel and of `investigation.html`'s Tab 1 (renamed from "Season Progress" to "Climate Risk," copy rewritten to match) both confirm clean rendering. The actual live Pyodide load-and-run in a real browser still needs checking before trusting it in front of students, the same standing caveat as every Pyodide-dependent feature in this file.

Remaining checklist items (#2-8) not yet started as of this entry -- see the numbered list above for what each covers; work through them in order unless told otherwise.

Pedagogical checklist paused; validation/transparency work for the CYCLES devs took priority (2026-09-22): Matt met with Armen and the CYCLES developers. Outcome, in his own words: "went reasonably well," but they are "skeptical about how ours functions and what assumptions it makes," and they declined to share the five items requested earlier this session (the `fwc` formula, bare-soil/residue evaporation, the pasture cutting trigger, wheat's water-stress anomaly, Saxton-Rawls secondary parameters -- all still open). Matt's direct instruction: build one page exposing this engine's full functionality with this platform's own UI, and produce an honest, systematic comparison against native Cycles output acknowledging where and why this reimplementation falls short -- before resuming the checklist above.

Real finding made while preparing that comparison, corrects this file's own prior record: the "unresolved mystery" flagged earlier this session (a corrupted scratch harvest.txt reproducing a different result, 10.56 Mg/ha mean / 0.540 correlation, than the historically-documented 10.93/0.771 for continuous corn) is now resolved, not still open. Checked directly: three independently-timestamped native Cycles runs against the same committed inputs (two archived clean copies from 2026-09-11, plus the current output directory) are byte-for-byte identical (md5 `24f4e7e0c3feec92bffe7d694e97e973`), and only the file explicitly named `_corrupted_backup` differs. **The correct, reproducible reference for continuous corn is mean 10.56 Mg/ha, correlation 0.540 -- not 10.93/0.771.** The 0.771 figure this file has quoted since 2026-09-10 was computed against a since-corrupted reference and was wrong. Corn does NOT clear the classroom-workable bar (~0.6-0.7) on the corrected number; of the five crops/rotations validated so far, only soybean (0.846) actually passes. Corrected everywhere this mattered: `SPECIES_REGISTRY.corn` in `engine-demo.html` (notes field now states the correction and the real number), and `engine-demo.html`'s own header copy (no longer claims corn "already tracks real Cycles output well"). `run_validation.py` itself needed no code change -- it was already computing against the correct (now-confirmed) reference file; only this file's own prose was stale.

Built `agricultural/prototypes/model-validation.html`, a new page distinct from `engine-demo.html` and `investigation.html` -- audience is the CYCLES developers and other technical reviewers, not students, and it says so in its own header. Two parts:
- **Full simulation controls**: one form exposing every parameter `simulate_season()` actually accepts, including six mechanisms that had zero UI anywhere in this project until now -- nitrogen application timing/splits (dated fall/spring applications instead of one lump), previous-crop nitrogen credit, manure amendment (real 0.5 availability multiplier), irrigation (trigger fraction + amount), tillage (the same real 5-implement curated subset used elsewhere, plus an exposed boost-window parameter), and planting-date override. Crop choice is read from the full `SPECIES_REGISTRY` (corn, corn_long_season, soybean, wheat, winter_rye, hairy_vetch -- every species with a real Python dict, not just the two offered as cash-crop choices elsewhere), each option labeled with its real correlation or data status inline. Site selection (preset + KML upload) reuses `resolveSiteAt()`/`parseFieldKML()`/`PRESET_SITES` verbatim from `engine-demo.html`, extracted and pasted rather than re-derived, to guarantee identical behavior. `ENGINE_SOURCE` is also reused verbatim (byte-identical extraction, confirmed by executing it directly in CPython before embedding); this page does not carry the ~600KB embedded Rock-Springs-only weather blob `engine-demo.html` uses, since every run here goes through a resolved real site instead -- a smaller, purpose-built glue layer (`GLUE_SOURCE`, just `to_row`/`build_season_rows`) replaces `engine-demo.html`'s `DEMO_GLUE_SOURCE`. A raw-JSON toggle shows the exact `simulate_season()` return dict for a technical reader who wants to see the actual output shape rather than a rendered summary.
- **Validation against real Cycles output**: a table with the corrected numbers for all five attempted crops/rotations (soybean 0.846 passes; corn 0.540, silage corn 0.501, winter wheat 0.253 all below bar; pasture perennials unresolved, no Python implementation exists), stating plainly that only one of five currently clears the bar. Below it, 11 `<details>` disclosures: the five items the devs declined to answer, plus six more structural gaps found independently while building this page and cross-referencing the engine's own code -- most consequentially, that curve-number runoff is implemented as a function (with a disclosed sign-error correction against the SI's typeset equations) but is **never actually called anywhere in the water balance** -- confirmed by grep, all precipitation goes straight to infiltration with zero runoff subtracted, meaning topography/slope has zero effect on anything in this model today. Also disclosed: every soil layer always initializes to field capacity with no spin-up or carryover (the exact question Matt asked just before this exchange); tillage's real six-pool decomposition mechanism isn't implemented (only a disclosed background-N stand-in sized by a real per-implement coefficient); no ammonia volatilization pathway exists at all (confirmed by grepping the extracted SI text for "volatiliz" -- zero matches, so injected vs. broadcast fertilization has no loss mechanism to model even though the real Cycles data distinguishes them via `till.txt`'s implement table); and the real sub-daily capacitance-weighted water redistribution physics (Eq. 1-2) is approximated by a same-day cascading bucket.

Verified before shipping, same discipline as every other Pyodide-dependent feature in this file: (1) the extracted `ENGINE_SOURCE`/`GLUE_SOURCE` strings execute cleanly in plain CPython (`py_compile` plus a real `exec()`, confirming `CROPS` and `build_season_rows` come out correctly formed) -- not just copy-pasted and assumed correct; (2) a full CPython run of the exact Python body `runFullSimulation()` sends to Pyodide, against real Central Iowa STATSGO2 soil and real NLDAS-2 2012 weather (via the same from-scratch `resolveSiteAt()` Python port built earlier this session), exercising every new mechanism at once and checking real physical invariants: a nitrogen total split across two dated applications reproduces the exact same yield as an equivalent single lump (differs only in leaching, as it should); 200 kg/ha of manure at the real 0.5 availability produces an identical result to 100 kg/ha of mineral fertilizer; irrigation and moldboard tillage each measurably raise yield at a nitrogen-limiting rate; a planting-date override is honored exactly; soybean's yield is completely unaffected by nitrogen rate (0 vs. 650 kg/ha, identical to machine precision) and carries no nitrogen-tracking fields in its output at all. (3) Headless Chromium against the real page with only `pyodide.runPythonAsync`/`globals.set` mocked (`resolveSiteAt()` untouched, so a real KML/preset click makes a real fetch against the real committed `weather-tiles/`/`statsgo2_soil_grid.json.gz`) confirmed: the crop dropdown lists all 6 real species with correct correlation/status labels, all 5 preset site buttons and 5 tillage options render, the validation table has all 7 rows and the gaps section has all 11 disclosures, clicking Central Iowa resolves real soil/weather and populates a real 37-year dropdown, the nitrogen-split/irrigation/planting-override checkboxes correctly show and hide their respective fields, a run correctly captures the intended nitrogen-application-pair global, the result headline/mass-balance line/raw-JSON toggle all render correctly, and zero JS console errors. A full-page screenshot confirmed clean rendering matching this unit's established visual language. Deliberately not linked from `index.html` or from `investigation.html` -- like the WASM-Cycles demo, this is a direct-link asset for a specific technical audience (the CYCLES developers), not a page general site visitors should land on.

Multi-variable validation pass, prompted by a direct Matt question (2026-09-22, same day): asked whether "0.54 correlation" for corn means the model is "off by 46%," and asked for validation tests across multiple variables to show how close/far this engine actually is. Answer to the direct question: no -- correlation and percent error are different statistics measuring different things, and conflating them understates how close the model actually gets on magnitude while overstating how well it tracks year-to-year swings. Corn grain's real mean-absolute-percent-error is 8.5%, not 46% -- the 0.540 figure is a correlation coefficient (does the model call the same years better/worse as real Cycles, on a -1 to +1 scale), not a percent-error measure at all.

Built a real multi-variable validation script (`/tmp` scratch, reusing `cycles_engine_validate.py`/`run_validation.py`/`run_validation_rotation2.py` verbatim, not reimplemented) comparing this engine against real Cycles output across 6 variables, not just final yield: grain yield, total biomass, aboveground biomass, harvest index, planting date, and reference ET (day-by-day, corn 2012), plus the already-covered soybean/wheat/silage-corn grain numbers. One real, previously-undocumented finding came out of this: **total biomass is a substantially worse miss than the reported grain yield** -- 29.1% mean error and 0.603 correlation, both worse than grain's 8.5%/0.540 (aboveground biomass sits in between, 11.3%/0.585, since the real shoot/root partitioning equation, Eq. SI.8-11, corrects some but not all of it). This makes sense once stated plainly: `calibration_factor` is fitted at the grain stage specifically (`grain = ag_biomass * HI * 10 * calibration_factor`), so a good grain match can and does mask a worse upstream biomass number -- grain looks more accurate than the model underneath it actually is. Harvest index is the clearest illustration of the correlation-vs-error distinction: its average level is close (6.1% mean error) but which particular years get a higher or lower ratio is close to uncorrelated (0.099) -- a real case of "close on average magnitude, wrong on which years are which."
