Planting date (2.1% error, 0.824 correlation) and reference ET (0.4% error, 1.000 correlation, a standard external FAO-56 formula) are this model's two strongest results by a wide margin.

Full table (real mean / model mean / mean % error / correlation): grain yield 10.56/10.96 Mg/ha, 8.5%, 0.540; total biomass 24.30/31.20 Mg/ha, 29.1%, 0.603; AG biomass 21.02/23.23 Mg/ha, 11.3%, 0.585; harvest index 0.501/0.472, 6.1%, 0.099; planting date 121.7/119.2 doy, 2.1%, 0.824; reference ET 2.81/2.82 mm/day, 0.4%, 1.000. Soybean grain 5.0% error/0.846 corr, silage corn forage 9.2%/0.501, winter wheat grain 12.1%/0.253 (all previously documented as correlation only, now with percent error added for the same close/far comparison). Added both a corrected main table (percent error column added, "correlation and percent error measure two different things" note added directly above it) and a new second table ("Beyond final yield: how the model's other outputs compare") to `model-validation.html`'s validation section, verified rendering cleanly in headless Chromium (2 tables, 7 rows each, zero JS errors) and via screenshot.

Four-scenario "just for fun" comparison, then a real head-to-head against native Cycles (2026-09-22, same day): Matt asked for four varied scenarios (location, crop, fertilizer, tillage) run through our own model as a sanity check. Ran them, then checked each against general knowledge (approximate recalled real-world average yields, not independently verified) rather than real Cycles output -- Matt correctly pushed back that this wasn't what he'd asked for. The actual fix: real Cycles binaries (`Cycles`/`Cycles_norelro`) are still present in this sandbox from earlier sessions and still run directly here. Built real Cycles `.weather`/`.soil`/`.operation`/`.ctrl` input files using the exact same resolved Iowa/Kansas STATSGO2 soil and NLDAS-2 weather this engine already uses (via the from-scratch `resolveSiteAt()` Python port), and ran native Cycles against them -- a true same-input head-to-head, not a comparison to memory. One real setup bug caught by Cycles' own warning, not silently wrong: `YEAR` in an `.operation` file is position-within-`ROTATION_SIZE`, not calendar year -- `YEAR 2` with `ROTATION_SIZE 1` silently skips the operation; fixed to `YEAR 1` (the operation then applies every simulated year, matching the bundled sample's own convention).

Six real Cycles-vs-our-model comparisons, same inputs, 2012 grain yield: Iowa corn N=150 (ours 5.11, Cycles 3.334 Mg/ha, +53%); Kansas corn N=150 (ours 2.45, Cycles **0.137**, **~18x**); Iowa soybean (ours 2.27, Cycles 1.146, +98%); Iowa corn N=50 (ours 3.25, Cycles 3.334, -2.5%, closest match); Iowa corn N=400 (ours 5.82, Cycles 3.334 -- identical to N=50/150, real Cycles shows ZERO nitrogen response at any tested rate for this site/year); Iowa corn N=50 with moldboard tillage (ours 3.53 vs. our own no-till 3.25, tillage HELPS; Cycles 2.850 vs. its own no-till 3.334, tillage HURTS -- opposite signs, not just different magnitudes). Real Cycles' own flat nitrogen response (identical grain at N=50/150/400) is the tell: that only happens when water, not nitrogen, is capping growth so hard nothing else matters -- meaning our model isn't as water-stressed as real Cycles is on the identical weather record. Root cause, confirmed by instrumenting the water balance directly (see the item-6/item-10 gap-list updates in `model-validation.html`): every run starts every soil layer at field capacity with zero carryover, and curve-number runoff, though implemented, was never called -- both push the same direction (this engine retains more water than reality, especially costly in dry climates). The tillage sign-flip has its own distinct cause: `mix_tilled_layers()` only ever redistributes moisture, never removes it, so it structurally cannot reproduce tillage's real drought downside (surface disturbance increasing evaporative loss) -- logged as a real, not-yet-fixed structural gap, not just a magnitude miss.

Acted on it the same day, per Matt's "start on 1 and 2 [runoff, spin-up] and see where that gets us": wired `runoff_mm()`/`moisture_adjusted_cn()` into `simulate_season()`'s water balance for real (added `compute_fwc()`, which never existed -- `infiltrate()` is the new shared runoff+redistribute helper, used by both the main day loop and the new optional `spinup_rows` parameter). `curve_number=75.0` (Cycles' own Rock Springs sample default, no real per-STATSGO2-cell source exists) and `slope_pct=0.0` (no real slope data anywhere in this project, as already disclosed) are the new flat defaults. `spinup_rows`, when given, runs a bare-soil (no canopy, no transpiration) water balance over real prior weather before the tracked season starts, replacing the always-full reset; `None` (default) reproduces the exact old behavior byte-for-byte. `run_validation.py`/`run_validation_rotation2.py` updated to pass each year's own real Jan-1-through-day-before-planting weather (already loaded, no new data needed -- generalizes correctly to fall-planted wheat too, since it keys off `plant_year`/`plant_doy`, not a fixed calendar assumption) as the spin-up window.

Honest result: **zero movement** in the only numbers with real ground truth. Corn 0.540, soybean 0.846, wheat 0.253 unchanged to three decimal places; silage corn 0.501->0.502 (noise). Diagnosed why rather than just reporting the null result: runoff is real and correctly triggers (verified directly, instrumented day-by-day) but Rock Springs' 2012 record's two genuinely heavy single-day storms (>18mm, the real curve-number trigger threshold at this soil's typical moisture) both fell after the modeled harvest date that year -- physically correct behavior for this specific weather record, not a bug. Spin-up is real and correctly dries the top soil layer (instrumented: theta dropped from field capacity to wilting point at Kansas after a year of bare-soil evaporation) but layers 2 and 3 never moved AT ALL, staying frozen exactly at field capacity through a full extra year of spin-up, because bare-soil evaporation physically only reaches the surface -- no roots exist during spin-up to pull water from depth. Tested directly against Kansas: a full extra year of spin-up moved grain from 2.451 to 2.450 Mg/ha, essentially nothing, confirming this mechanism cannot close the Kansas-scale gap regardless of window length. Conclusion, stated plainly rather than softened: neither fix moves our validated numbers, because Rock Springs is humid enough that neither missing-runoff nor missing-antecedent-dryness was ever load-bearing there -- which also means the real bottleneck behind corn/wheat's weak correlation is NOT on the water-input side of the balance, and closing the Kansas-type gap for real needs genuine multi-year, crop-inclusive state carryover (an actual prior crop's roots actually depleting the profile across real seasons), a real subsystem to build, not a patch. Both changes kept in the engine regardless (real, disclosed, opt-in completions of two previously-flagged gaps) and `model-validation.html`'s gap-list items 6 and 10 updated with this full account rather than left as open questions now answered by silence.

Deriving the water-stress response curve directly from real Cycles output, then reverting it (2026-09-22, same day, per Matt's direct "look at the water-stress response curve's shape... is it possible to derive [it] by running multiple scenarios with our model and cycles and figuring out what the factor is"): pushed back on the proposed method before building anything -- comparing aggregate season-level outcomes (like grain yield) across scenarios to infer ONE internal function's shape would confound it with RUE, WUE, canopy timing, root depth, and N stress all changing at once, making the one function nearly impossible to isolate cleanly. A more direct path exists: real Cycles publishes both ingredients this function needs as its own daily output -- a WATER STRESS column (`CornRM.90.txt`) and per-layer soil moisture (`water.txt`, SMC by layer) -- so the two can be paired directly, no back-solving through yield needed at all.

Built this pairing across the full real 37-year Rock Springs continuous-corn record: for every day the crop was actively growing (stages VEGETATIVE_GROWTH/REPRODUCTIVE_GROWTH/PRE_EMERGENCE, confirmed via real column values, not guessed), reconstructed a root-zone available-water fraction from Cycles' own reported per-layer SMC (via `saxton_rawls()`-derived real FC/PWP for the real Rock Springs soil texture, this engine's own `root_zone_availability()` function reused as-is, and this engine's own root-depth-vs-thermal-time assumption applied to Cycles' own reported thermal time -- held fixed deliberately so the root-depth question isn't conflated with the stress-function question) and paired it against Cycles' own reported WATER STRESS value for that exact date (converted from Cycles' 0%=none/100%=full convention to this engine's 1.0=none/0=full multiplier). One real column-indexing near-miss caught before trusting any of it: counted `WATER STRESS`'s real position in `CornRM.90.txt`'s header (1-indexed column 18) two different ways during a first draft and got column 17 (N STRESS) by mistake -- caught by explicitly printing and counting the tab-separated header before writing the parsing code, not after.

4928 real (avail_frac, stress) pairs, binned and eyeballed first: at low avail_frac (0.10-0.30), real Cycles is far LESS severe than this engine's current linear ramp assumes (e.g. at avail_frac~0.125, real Cycles' mean stress-multiplier is 0.575 -- only 42% stressed -- vs. the linear ramp's 0.25, i.e. 75% stressed). A real least-squares grid search over a threshold/power family (`stress = min(1, (avail_frac/threshold)^power)`) against the raw unbinned pairs, not just binned means, found a clean best fit: threshold=0.45, power=0.51 -- essentially `sqrt(avail_frac/0.45)` -- cutting squared error from 79.3 (the current plain linear ramp) to 31.5, a genuine ~60% reduction, and a physically recognizable concave shape (consistent with real soil-hydraulic-conductance/plant-water-uptake relationships in the literature), not an arbitrary curve-fit artifact.

Also caught and fixed a real, freshly-introduced inconsistency while in this code: `_reference_n_demand()` (the whole-season-nitrogen-demand helper added earlier this session) deliberately duplicates the main loop's water/canopy physics for auditability, but had NOT been updated when runoff was wired in two turns ago -- it still called the old bare `redistribute()` directly, meaning the two loops' water balances had silently diverged (a latent bug with no measurable effect yet, since runoff's total effect at Rock Springs was already shown to be negligible, but a real one for any future site with heavier storms). Fixed: `_reference_n_demand()` now takes the same `curve_number`/`slope_pct`/`spinup_rows` parameters as `simulate_season()` and uses `infiltrate()`, threaded through from the one call site. The plain linear stress formula, previously duplicated inline in both loops (a real drift risk this project has been bitten by before, see the engineering unit's own "don't let a default exist in two places" lesson), was also pulled into one new named function, `water_stress_response()`, used by both.

Plugged the fitted curve into `water_stress_response()` and re-ran the full validation suite. Honest result: correlation got WORSE for every one of the four validated crops -- corn 0.540->0.533, soybean 0.846->0.823, wheat 0.253->0.210, silage corn 0.501/0.502->0.495. Diagnosed rather than just reported: the fit was built from Cycles' own real soil-moisture trajectory, which comes from its real sub-daily capacitance-weighted redistribution physics (Eq. 1-2) -- but this engine approximates that with a same-day cascading bucket (an already-disclosed, still-unresolved gap, see `QUESTIONS_FOR_DEVS.md` and the Saxton-Rawls-secondary-parameters item). The two models' avail_frac trajectories don't move through a season the same way, so a curve tuned to map real Cycles' avail_frac to real Cycles' stress doesn't necessarily transfer well when fed this engine's differently-behaved avail_frac. **Reverted** rather than shipped: `water_stress_response()` now holds the original plain linear ramp (verified byte-for-byte reproduction of the pre-experiment correlation numbers for all four crops after reverting), with the full derivation, the fitted parameters, and the negative result recorded in its own docstring so this isn't re-attempted blind. Real, useful takeaway despite the revert: the water-stress response curve's shape is probably not the dominant lever behind corn/wheat's weak correlation -- the water-redistribution physics upstream of it (the cascading-bucket approximation itself) is the more likely place to look next. Also added as gap-list item 12 in `model-validation.html`, matching this project's standing discipline of documenting a real, well-diagnosed negative result as fully as a positive one.

"What would real modelers do" and a real breakthrough via provided sources (2026-09-22, same day): Matt pushed back directly on the whole approach so far -- reading one 2024 paper closely and guessing/fitting when it's silent isn't how modelers actually respond to a challenged model, and Kemanian et al. built on real prior intellectual work, not from nothing. Used WebSearch (reachable in this sandbox; WebFetch itself is blocked for every domain tried, same blanket egress policy as everywhere else) to trace that lineage before touching any code: confirmed Cycles descends from C-Farm (Kemanian & Stöckle 2010) and shares its biophysical core with CropSyst (Stöckle et al. 2014), and that Cycles' transpiration/water-stress mechanism is specifically "adapted from Campbell et al. 1974, Campbell 1985, and Jara & Stöckle 1998" -- real, named, findable sources fuller than the compressed 2024 SI, not alternatives to compare against. Also found a real documentation trail never checked before: a GitHub wiki (`github.com/PSUmodeling/Cycles/wiki`) and user guide (`psumodeling.github.io/Cycles/`), separate from the binary-only release repo already correctly ruled out for source code.

Matt then did what real modelers actually do when blocked from a source: fetched the blocked pages himself and handed them over, plus real reference PDFs (`KemanianStockle2010.pdf`, `overview-of-c-farm-dec-2008-web-document_2.pdf`, `Kemanianetal2024.pdf`, and a saved FAO-56 Chapter 8 mhtml, all committed directly to `main`, alongside unrelated CAMEL/materials-science content added in the same upload that this session left untouched, not ours to touch). The two C-Farm papers turned out to cover only the carbon submodel in equation form -- confirmed by reading both in full (the 2010 journal paper via the Read tool's native PDF rendering, the 2008 overview via pdftotext) -- water balance is only described qualitatively there ("new sub-routines... two-stage soil water evaporation... without resorting to a numerical solution"), not with equations. One real, usable find from that reading, logged for later even though not used today: C-Farm's own real, disclosed tillage-decomposition-acceleration formula, `ft = ftx*dr/(dr+exp(5.5-0.05*dr))` with `ftx = 1+4*exp(-5.5*fclay)` -- a genuine alternative to this engine's own ad hoc tillage-boost stand-in, not pursued this session since it wasn't what was blocking anything.

The FAO-56 mhtml was the real breakthrough. Decoded it directly (Python's `email` module parses MHTML as a MIME message; the HTML part crudely tag-stripped to text) and found the actual water-stress coefficient chapter Matt had also gotten via a search summary the previous turn -- extracted this time, with an important, checkable result: **this engine's existing plain linear water-stress ramp already IS FAO-56's real, standard Ks formula (Allen et al. 1998, Eq. 84)**, not an invented shape. Verified by reproducing the chapter's own worked numeric example (Example 37, Day 3 and Day 10) to the given precision from the extracted text alone -- Ks=(TAW-Dr)/(TAW-RAW), which in this engine's avail_frac terms reduces exactly to avail_frac/(1-p). What was actually wrong wasn't the shape, it was the threshold: this engine defaulted every crop to p=0.50 (a literature-guessed constant), but FAO-56's own real Table 22 gives specific per-crop values -- maize (field, grain) and both spring/winter wheat at p=0.55, soybeans at p=0.50 (exactly matching the old default, a clean built-in sanity check). Strikingly, 0.45 (=1-0.55) is the EXACT threshold the earlier same-day reverted power-law fit found empirically -- that attempt had the right threshold and the wrong shape (a concave curve where the real relationship is linear), an artifact of grid-searching threshold and power together and letting a better-fitting wrong-shape combination win over a correct-shape, real-threshold one that wasn't in the search's best-fit corner.

Implemented as a per-crop `depletion_fraction` field (real Table 22 values) rather than a shared constant, threaded through `water_stress_response()`'s new second parameter (both call sites, main loop and `_reference_n_demand`, default 0.50 when a crop doesn't set one -- fully backward-compatible). Re-ran the full validation suite. Honest, mixed result: corn 0.540->0.544 and silage corn 0.501->0.511 both improved (small, real, not a regression, unlike this morning's attempt); soybean is byte-identical at 0.846 as predicted (its real p already matched the old default, the sanity check held); winter wheat got WORSE, 0.253->0.226. Kept the real, sourced p=0.55 for wheat anyway rather than quietly reverting to a better-scoring but unsourced value just for that one crop -- consistent with, not contradicted by, wheat's already-separately-diagnosed anomaly (its real reported stress is far larger than its real soil moisture depletion would explain by any standard function, see the "Follow-up diagnostic session" paragraph earlier in this file and `QUESTIONS_FOR_DEVS.md` item 7) -- this fix answers a different, now-closed question (the right generic threshold) and doesn't touch the separate, still-open one (wheat's specific extra stress mechanism). Documented fully as an update to `model-validation.html`'s gap-list item 12, including an explicit, honest flag that this page's own live "Full simulation controls" panel has NOT been ported to match yet (a deliberate scope decision, same as the runoff/spin-up work earlier the same day) -- the static validation table and the page's own interactive engine will disagree until that porting happens, stated plainly rather than left to silently drift.

Second sourced lead from the same exchange, acted on the next day (2026-09-23): the same message where Matt supplied FAO-56 also gave the real SCS Antecedent Soil Moisture Condition formulas verbatim (CN1/CN3 from CN2, sourced from SWAT+ documentation, Eq 2:1.1.4-1.1.5) -- a different sub-piece of the same curve-number runoff mechanism than the FAO-56 fix above, not a duplicate lead. This engine already had `cn_dry()`/`cn_wet()` implementing a *different* standard formula family (a rounded NEH-4-style approximation, unsourced in code beyond "standard form"), not Matt's exact equations. Checked numerically before swapping: both formula families agree within about 1-2.5 CN points across a realistic 50-95 CN2 range, confirming both are legitimate, just different, standard approximations of the same conversion -- this was a refinement to the exact, citable formula, not a correction of something wrong. Replaced `cn_dry()`/`cn_wet()` with Matt's exact SWAT+ formulas, cited directly in the docstring. Re-ran the full validation suite: corn (0.544), wheat (0.226), and silage corn (0.511) correlations unchanged to three decimals; soybean's mean absolute error moved 0.24->0.23 Mg/ha, correlation unchanged at 0.846 -- essentially no effect, consistent with the already-documented finding (2026-09-22, the runoff/spin-up wiring session) that curve-number runoff barely triggers before harvest in the Rock Springs record regardless of exactly which AMC endpoint formula feeds it. Kept anyway since it's the more precisely sourced formula and costs nothing. Updated `model-validation.html`'s gap-list item 1 (the `fwc` disclosure) with this result, distinguishing it clearly from `fwc` itself, which remains the separate, still-undisclosed piece of this same mechanism. Not yet ported to the embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE` copies in `engine-demo.html`/`model-validation.html` -- confirmed those copies don't have `cn_dry`/`cn_wet` at all yet (the runoff-wiring work itself was only ever done in the canonical scripts), so this doesn't create any new divergence beyond what's already flagged.

FAO-56 depletion-fraction fix ported into the embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE` copies (2026-09-23), closing the divergence flagged the day before: `engine-demo.html` and `model-validation.html` had been running the old flat `avail_frac/0.5` water-stress ramp inline at both call sites (never refactored into a named function there, unlike the canonical script), with no `depletion_fraction` field on any crop dict. Ported `water_stress_response(avail_frac, depletion_fraction=0.5)` into both embedded copies verbatim, replaced both inline call sites with `water_stress_response(avail_frac, crop.get("depletion_fraction", 0.5))`, and added the real FAO-56 Table 22 values to each file's `CORN` (0.55), `SOYBEAN` (0.50), and `WHEAT` (0.55) dicts -- `CORN_LONG_SEASON` inherits corn's value automatically (`dict(CORN)`), and `WINTER_RYE`/`HAIRY_VETCH` correctly fall through to the unchanged 0.50 default via `.get()`, matching how the canonical script treats those same two species (no depletion_fraction defined for either there either).

Verified by extracting each file's `ENGINE_SOURCE` and running it in plain CPython against the real embedded Rock Springs weather: both files' `ENGINE_SOURCE` strings are now byte-identical to each other (confirmed via diff, as they should be, since they only ever start from a byte-identical extraction, this file's own standing practice). Soybean's 2012 N=0 grain (4.362 Mg/ha) is unchanged to three decimals, matching the already-documented Tab 3 crop-compare number exactly -- direct confirmation the port is correct, not just plausible, since soybean's real p already equalled the old default. Corn's 2012 N=650 grain legitimately moved from the old 10.4987 to 10.7248 Mg/ha, a real, expected shift (the same direction the canonical script's own corn correlation improved, 0.540->0.544) -- not a bug, and not reverted.

**Real, disclosed consequence worth flagging plainly, not smoothed over**: dozens of specific corn/wheat numbers already written into this file's own history for `engine-demo.html`'s panels (the Tab 3 crossover point at 37.22 kg N/ha, the profit-maximizing rate at 350 kg N/ha, the tillage-compare spread percentages, the season-risk 37-year averages, the nitrogen-balance mass-splits, and more) were all computed against the OLD flat 0.50 threshold and will no longer reproduce exactly if re-run against the now-ported engine. The qualitative lessons each panel teaches are not expected to break (a modest threshold refinement doesn't flip a real crossover into no-crossover, or turn diminishing returns into constant returns), but none of those specific historical numbers should be quoted as currently-reproducible without re-running the panel first. Re-deriving every one of them was out of scope for this port -- flagged here rather than silently left to drift, consistent with this file's own "disclose the gap" discipline. Re-verify a given panel's specific numbers at the point they're next touched, not before.

Verified via headless Chromium (this sandbox's standing Pyodide-CDN limitation applies as always) that neither file's HTML/JS structure was damaged by the string-replacement edits: both pages load with correct titles and sane panel counts (13 for `engine-demo.html`, 4 for `model-validation.html`), and the only console errors present are the same pre-existing CDN-unreachable `loadPyodide is not defined` chain documented everywhere else in this file, not anything newly introduced.

Tillage's decomposition-boost mechanism replaced with C-Farm's real, disclosed formula (2026-09-23, same day, the next fix in the same "act like real modelers" thread): the tillage-boost stand-in had used an arbitrary flat `1+mixing_efficiency` multiplier held constant for a hardcoded `tillage_boost_days=30` window -- both numbers invented, not sourced. The exact real mechanism (Kemanian & Stockle 2010, C-Farm's own Eq. 5: `ft = ftx*dr/(dr+exp(5.5-0.05*dr))`, `ftx = 1+4*exp(-5.5*fclay)`) was already found and read in full the day before but not yet acted on. Confirmed exactly what its `dr` term means before implementing anything: a cumulative soil-disturbance rating on the real 0-30 NRCS Soil Conditioning Index scale, additive per tillage operation, decaying at up to 2%/day at field capacity (slower when drier) -- and confirmed a genuinely useful, previously-flagged-as-unused connection: this project's own `till.txt` already carries a real `SOIL_DISTURB_RATIO` per implement (`Plow_moldboard`=29, `Fert_applic_surface_broadcast`=5, matching the paper's own stated 25-29 "aggressive" / low "gentle" ranges exactly) -- flagged as unused in this file's own 2026-09-21 entry, now with a real use.

Scoped deliberately short of the real full mechanism: Eq. 5's `ft` is a term inside a complete soil-carbon differential equation (per-layer Cs pools, humification, saturation dynamics) this engine doesn't implement and isn't in scope to build (a genuine new subsystem, not a parameter swap, exactly as this file's own 2026-09-18 assessment already flagged tillage as needing). What was built instead: `ft`'s real shape (rises sharply right after tillage, decays smoothly toward 0, texture-dependent via `ftx`) applied as the real, sourced replacement for the old arbitrary boxcar multiplier on `BACKGROUND_N_KG_HA_DAY` -- same disclosed-stand-in framing as before, now driven by real numbers and a real decay curve instead of two invented constants. `TILLAGE_IMPLEMENTS` gained a third element (`soil_disturb_rating`, re-parsed directly from the real `till.txt`, all 86 entries, zero mismatches against the existing depth/mixing_efficiency values already there) in `cycles_engine_validate.py`; new `tillage_ftx()`, `tillage_ft()`, `tillage_dr_decay()` functions implement the real formula and a disclosed (not verified) moisture-scaling interpretation of the paper's own "2%/day at field capacity" statement. `_reference_n_demand()` and `simulate_season()` both track a running `dr` state (jumps by the tilled implement's real rating on `tillage_doy`, decays daily) and apply `1+ft` in place of the old fixed multiplier; `tillage_boost_days` is gone entirely, replaced by a new `tillage_clay_frac=0.21` parameter (Rock Springs' own real topsoil clay fraction as the default) feeding `ftx`'s real texture scaling.

Verified: `run_validation.py`/`run_validation_rotation2.py` reproduce all four crops' correlations unchanged to three decimals (corn 0.544, soybean 0.846, wheat 0.226, silage corn 0.511) -- the `tillage_doy=None` default path is untouched. The new mechanism passes the same sanity check this project always uses for tillage: a real, substantial yield gain at a nitrogen-limiting rate (2012 corn, N=50, moldboard plow: +0.67 Mg/ha, actually larger than the old mechanism's +0.42, since `ftx`≈2.26 is a stronger real boost right after tillage than the old flat multiplier was) and ~zero effect at a non-limiting rate (N=650: identical to four decimal places). An unrecognized implement name still raises `ValueError`. The decay timescale independently lands close to the paper's own stated "~90 days" fade (a hand-computed trajectory assuming near-field-capacity moisture throughout: `ft` drops from 0.76 right after a moldboard-plow pass to 0.05 by day 90) -- a real, if imperfect, cross-check that the moisture-scaling interpretation is in the right ballpark, not verified exact.

Real, pre-existing, live-breaking bug found and fixed while porting this into the embedded copies, unrelated to today's mechanism change: `engine-demo.html`/`model-validation.html`'s embedded `TILLAGE_IMPLEMENTS` had only ever carried a 43-entry subset of the real 86-implement catalog, not the full table like the canonical script -- and three of the five real implements the UI's own `TILLAGE_OPTIONS` advertises (`Plow_moldboard`, `Chisel_sweep_shovel`, `Cultivator_field_w_spike_points`) were never in that subset at all. Confirmed directly by executing the actual embedded engine: selecting "Moldboard plow," "Chisel plow," or "Field cultivator" in any of the three live panels that use tillage (field-comparison, nitrogen-sweep, rotation) would throw a real `ValueError` in the browser -- only "Disk, tandem" (and "No tillage") actually worked. This contradicts this file's own earlier verification claims for the tillage-UI work (2026-09-18, "confirmed Plow_moldboard measurably raises yield... in the field-comparison panel's code path") -- that verification was evidently run against the canonical script's full dict, not the actually-embedded subset, an overclaim worth remembering the shape of: CPython-testing "the embedded engine" only proves what it was actually run against, not what's genuinely in the shipped page, unless the exact embedded string is what's executed (the discipline this file otherwise holds to, but evidently didn't fully in that one round). Fixed by replacing the embedded 43-entry subset with the full, real 86-entry table in both files (matching the canonical script exactly) -- removes this whole class of risk going forward rather than patching the missing three.

Ported into both embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE` copies and verified byte-identical to each other afterward (confirmed via diff): the three new helper functions, the `TILLAGE_IMPLEMENTS` 3-tuple extension, and the `_reference_n_demand()`/`simulate_season()` rewrites. One real scoping decision made along the way: the embedded copies' `_reference_n_demand()` was already simpler than the canonical script's (no `curve_number`/`slope_pct`/`spinup_rows` parameters at all -- the runoff/spin-up work from 2026-09-22 was never ported there either, already flagged separately). Porting today's tillage fix onto that simpler existing structure, without also porting runoff/spin-up in the same pass, was a deliberate scope choice -- pulling in an unrelated engine upgrade would have widened this fix well beyond what was asked. Re-verified the ported copies directly: the same moldboard-plow sanity check (N=50: +0.68 Mg/ha; N=650: ~0.0000) reproduces correctly through both `engine-demo.html`'s own `DEMO_GLUE_SOURCE` and `model-validation.html`'s own `GLUE_SOURCE`/`resolveSiteAt()` path.

`model-validation.html`'s UI updated to match: removed the now-meaningless "Boost window (days after tillage)" number input entirely (the real formula's own decay replaces the need for a caller-set window) and its JS wiring; the outer glue now derives `tillage_clay_frac` from the resolved site's own real topsoil clay percentage (`_fc_soil_raw[0]["clay"]/100.0`) instead of the flat 0.21 default, a small real accuracy improvement for non-Rock-Springs sites that cost nothing since that soil data was already resolved for the run. Verified via headless Chromium: the removed field is gone from the DOM, the tillage `<select>` still renders all 5 real options correctly.

Runoff/spin-up mechanism ported into the embedded copies too (2026-09-23, per Matt's direct follow-up), closing the last of the three divergences flagged over the previous two days' porting work. `engine-demo.html`/`model-validation.html` previously had none of `slope_factor()`/`cn_dry()`/`cn_wet()`/`moisture_adjusted_cn()`/`compute_fwc()`/`runoff_mm()`/`infiltrate()` at all -- both files' `simulate_season()` called bare `redistribute()` directly, meaning curve-number runoff and antecedent-moisture spin-up were canonical-script-only capabilities, never actually reachable from either live page. Ported the full mechanism (already carrying the real SWAT+ AMC-I/III formulas from 2026-09-23's earlier fix, not the superseded NEH-4 approximation) into both `ENGINE_SOURCE` copies, threaded `spinup_rows`/`curve_number`/`slope_pct` through `_reference_n_demand()` and `simulate_season()` exactly as in the canonical script, and added `runoff_mm` to the result dict.

One real, scoped consistency fix made along the way, not part of the canonical script at all: `engine-demo.html`'s own `bare_fallow_leaching()` (the rotation panel's bare-soil counterfactual, unique to this demo) also called bare `redistribute()` -- left alone, the rotation panel's cover-crop-vs-bare-fallow comparison would have had real runoff subtracted from one side (the actual crop phases, now running through `infiltrate()`) but not the other, an unfair comparison. Added the same `curve_number`/`slope_pct` parameters and switched it to `infiltrate()` too, keeping that panel's own A/B comparison fair.

Verified: both embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE` copies confirmed byte-identical to each other after the port (via diff, as always). The canonical script is untouched this round (`git diff --stat` empty) and its full validation suite reproduces the exact same numbers as after yesterday's fixes (corn 0.544, soybean 0.846, wheat 0.226, silage corn 0.511) -- expected, since only the embedded copies changed. Ran the real embedded engine in plain CPython: the 2012 corn N=650 reference number is byte-identical to yesterday's post-tillage-fix value (10.724839... Mg/ha) despite `runoff_mm` now correctly coming back nonzero (0.112mm) -- a small enough runoff that it doesn't cross any stress threshold in this exact case, consistent with (not contradicting) the canonical script's own earlier finding that curve-number runoff barely matters at Rock Springs. A real, non-zero spin-up effect was confirmed separately (2012 corn N=150: 7.968 Mg/ha with no spin-up vs. 7.956 with a real Jan-1-through-planting antecedent-moisture window -- small, real, and in the expected direction). The tillage sanity check (moldboard plow: real gain at N=50, ~zero at N=650) still reproduces correctly on top of the new runoff/spin-up code, confirming the two mechanisms compose without interfering. `bare_fallow_leaching()` runs cleanly with its new signature. Re-ran the identical checks through `model-validation.html`'s own `GLUE_SOURCE`/`resolveSiteAt()` path and got matching numbers.

`model-validation.html`'s gap-list items 1, 6, and 10 (the `fwc`/AMC-swap/runoff/spin-up disclosures) each got a short update note pointing out that what was previously true only of the canonical validation scripts now also applies to this page's own live engine -- no new UI controls added for `spinup_rows`/`curve_number`/`slope_pct` (both remain reachable via `simulate_season()`'s own defaults, not yet exposed as form fields), scoped deliberately to "wire the mechanism in," not "add new sliders," since that wasn't asked for this round. Verified via headless Chromium that both pages still load cleanly (correct titles, panel counts, only the standing pre-existing CDN-unreachable errors) and that the gap-list section's `<details>`/`<div>` tags remain balanced after the added update paragraphs.

Saxton-Rawls (2006) secondary hydraulic parameters obtained and implemented (2026-09-23, same day): after WebSearch failed to surface the exact equation text for these formulas (three targeted queries, including one that found a real USDA WEPS Fortran source file with matching variable names but no formula content -- confirming the network-policy block flagged in the gap list wasn't a search-skill problem), Matt asked for the full citation and independently obtained and uploaded the real paper (`soil-characteristics-by-texture-and-organic-rawls.pdf`, committed directly to `main`, same pattern as every other blocked source this project has needed). Read in full via the Read tool's native PDF rendering.

`saxton_rawls()` already implemented the paper's Eq. 1-3/5 (wilting point, field capacity, saturation) but none of its secondary parameters -- air-entry tension (Eq. 4), the pore-size distribution exponent B/&lambda; (Eq. 14-15/18), or saturated hydraulic conductivity Ksat (Eq. 16) -- despite these being exactly what the already-disclosed redistribution-physics gap (item 5/10 in `model-validation.html`'s gap list, referenced repeatedly since 2026-09-22) said was missing. Added all three as new return-dict fields (`psi_e_kpa`, `B`, `lam`, `ksat_mm_day`), verified before trusting by reproducing the paper's own Table 3 worked examples directly through the real function, not a scratch script: Sand texture (S=88%, C=5%, OM=2.5%w) reproduces the table's WP=5/FC=10/SAT=46 %v and Ksat=108.1 mm/h as 108.15; Loam (S=40%, C=20%) reproduces WP=14/FC=28/SAT=46 %v exactly and Ksat=15.5 mm/h as 15.48 -- both essentially exact, the same "reproduce the source's own numeric example" discipline that verified the FAO-56 depletion-fraction fix two days earlier.

Ksat is the one of the three with an immediate, real use: `redistribute()` (the same-day cascading-bucket water balance, already disclosed as an approximation of the paper's real sub-daily capacitance-weighted Eq. 1-2) previously let water move between layers at an unlimited daily rate regardless of soil texture -- a clay layer with a real Ksat of tens of mm/day behaved identically to a sandy layer with a real Ksat in the hundreds. Rewired `redistribute()` to rate-limit each layer's downward drainage to its own `ksat_mm_day` when present on the layer dict, letting water above field capacity now genuinely persist (capped at saturation) across days when the local conductivity is limiting, rather than always draining out same-day. A layer dict with no `ksat_mm_day` key falls back to the exact old unlimited-rate behavior -- verified directly with a synthetic two-case test (a rate-limited layer given ksat=20mm/day retains 10mm of a 30mm excess and passes 20mm through; the identical layer with no ksat key passes all 30mm through, matching pre-change behavior bit for bit).

Wired `ksat_mm_day` into `make_layers()` in both `run_validation.py` and `run_validation_rotation2.py`, then ran the full regression suite: **zero measurable effect** on any of the four validated crops, to three decimal places (corn 0.544, soybean 0.846, wheat 0.226, silage corn 0.511 -- all identical to the pre-change values). Diagnosed rather than left unexplained: Rock Springs' real per-layer Ksat values run 43-315 mm/day (checked directly), high enough relative to this site's real daily rainfall (the same record whose only two >18mm storms in 2012 both fell after harvest, per the runoff investigation two days earlier) that the rate cap essentially never binds. This is the third mechanism in three days (after curve-number runoff and antecedent-moisture spin-up) to be real, correctly implemented, and completely non-load-bearing at this specific site's climate -- not a pattern of the fixes being wrong, but real evidence that Rock Springs' humid climate isn't where this engine's remaining accuracy gap actually lives; the Kansas-type dry-soil gap and wheat's anomalous stress response (both already diagnosed as needing real multi-year state carryover, not a single-day water-balance refinement) remain the more likely places to look next.

Kept the change anyway (real, physically correct, harmless at Rock Springs, and could plausibly bind at a drier or heavier-storm site) and ported it into both embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE` copies identically, verified byte-identical to each other before and after (38398 -> 38430 bytes, the same +32-byte growth in both). Also wired `ksat_mm_day` into every `make_layers()`-equivalent across both HTML files' glue layers (8 call sites in `engine-demo.html`'s `ENGINE_SOURCE`+`DEMO_GLUE_SOURCE`, 2 in `model-validation.html`'s `ENGINE_SOURCE`+`GLUE_SOURCE`, one shared find-and-replace per file since all 8/2 sites share the identical `dict(thick=..., sat=hyd["sat"], theta=hyd["fc"]))` tail) -- deliberately proactive, since leaving some site-resolved soil profiles with real Ksat and others without would have been the exact same class of silent-divergence bug already caught once this session (`TILLAGE_IMPLEMENTS`' 43-vs-86-entry gap). Verified the extracted embedded engine executes cleanly end to end (`make_layers()` output confirmed to carry a real `ksat_mm_day` matching the canonical script's own value for Rock Springs' topsoil layer, 314.877 mm/day) and re-ran `run_validation.py`/`run_validation_rotation2.py` one final time after all edits, confirming the same four unchanged numbers.

Not done, and flagged honestly rather than implied: air-entry tension and &lambda; are computed and available but consumed by nothing yet -- a real prerequisite for a fuller unsaturated-flow (Darcy/Richards-style) redistribution scheme, not that scheme itself. Building the real sub-daily Eq. 1-2 integral would need Kemanian et al. 2024's own exact mathematical form for it (already have the PDF, haven't re-read it closely enough this round to transcribe), not just these Saxton-Rawls soil parameters -- a materially larger undertaking than this session's last several single-function formula swaps, not attempted here. `model-validation.html`'s gap-list item 5 (formerly "declined by devs") rewritten to state what's now resolved (the three parameters, verified) versus what remains open (the real redistribution scheme itself), rather than leaving the old text implying nothing had changed.

Real caveat worth stating plainly, same discipline as the last two porting rounds: every panel across both embedded pages now has curve-number runoff active by default (not opt-in -- `curve_number=75.0`/`slope_pct=0.0` are the flat defaults, same as canonical), where previously none of them did. Only the one Rock-Springs-2012-corn case above was directly checked and found to have zero measurable effect; the many other previously-documented panel numbers throughout this file (Iowa/Kansas comparisons, the season-risk sweep, the frontier chart, etc.) were not individually re-verified against this change, matching the same disclosed-not-exhaustive standard already applied to the water-stress and tillage ports. Spin-up itself remains fully opt-in (`spinup_rows=None` by default) and no existing panel passes it, so no panel's numbers shift from that half of this fix.

Real Eq. 1 (Kemanian et al. 2024) implemented via a closed-form derivation, replacing the flat Ksat rate cap from earlier the same day (2026-09-23, continuing the "next fix" thread): re-read the paper's own Section 2.4 closely rather than treating the sub-daily redistribution physics as a single monolithic "materially larger undertaking" to defer wholesale. The paper's own Eq. 1 defines khe, a capacitance-weighted effective hydraulic conductivity between a layer's current moisture and field capacity, as a ratio of two integrals over matric potential -- looking intractable at first glance, but it collapses to closed form once Campbell's (1974) own power-law moisture-release curve (theta(psi)=theta_sat*(psi_e/psi)^(1/b), the same curve family Saxton-Rawls' own B parameter describes) is substituted in: both the numerator and denominator become simple power-function integrals of theta, since d(theta)/d(psi) integrated over psi is just theta(psi_s)-theta(psi_sfc) by the fundamental theorem of calculus. Verified the derivation against `scipy.integrate.quad` brute-force numerical integration of the paper's own literal integral across several realistic soil-texture parameter sets, matching to ~1e-15 relative error.

A real correction was needed to trust the result: the paper's own printed exponent in the numerator, "(2+3b)", produced physically absurd near-zero conductivity everywhere except within a hair of saturation when read literally -- checked numerically, not just suspected, since b for real soils runs 4-10 and (psi_e/psi)^(2+3b) crushes to near-zero across almost the entire realistic moisture range. Reading it as "2+3/b" instead (a division slash very plausibly lost in the PDF's text extraction, the same class of typesetting/OCR issue already documented in this file for the SI's sign errors) exactly recovers Campbell's own independently well-known, textbook conductivity form K(theta)=Ksat*(theta/theta_sat)^(2b+3) -- both the numeric implausibility of the literal reading and the exact match to a standard, citable formula point the same direction, giving real confidence in the correction rather than picking whichever reading was more convenient.

Added `campbell_khe(theta_s, theta_sfc, theta_sat, ksat, psi_e, b)` to `cycles_engine_validate.py`, using it in `redistribute()` in place of today's earlier flat `ksat_mm_day` cap whenever a layer carries the full parameter set (`psi_e_kpa`, `B`), with the flat cap and then the original unlimited-rate behavior as successive fallbacks for a layer dict missing one or both fields -- the same three-tier graceful degradation pattern already used for the ksat cap itself. Verified in isolation before trusting it in the full engine: a layer just above field capacity now drains almost nothing in a day (0.2mm of a tiny excess), and even a layer near saturation doesn't fully drain back to field capacity in one day (ends partway, not snapped to field capacity) -- a real, qualitatively different behavior from the flat-ksat-cap version, which let any excess up to the flat rate drain out completely each day regardless of how close to field capacity the layer already was. This is the real physical behavior the paper's own prose describes ("weighting by capacitance slows down water flow as the soil approaches field capacity"), not an invented shape.

Wired `psi_e_kpa`/`B` into `make_layers()` in `run_validation.py`/`run_validation_rotation2.py` and ran the full validation suite. Honest, genuinely mixed result, the first real movement (in either direction) from any of this week's three prior water-balance mechanism additions (runoff, spin-up, flat Ksat rate-limiting, all of which measured exactly zero effect at Rock Springs): corn 0.544->0.546, soybean 0.846->0.851, silage corn 0.511->0.526, winter wheat 0.226->0.222. Three of four crops improved, modestly; wheat moved slightly the other way. Not a breakthrough -- these are small, single-digit-thousandths-to-low-hundredths movements, not a step change -- but structurally coherent rather than noise: silage corn, the one crop whose weak correlation was specifically diagnosed earlier this session as a water-balance *timing* problem tied to the redistribution-physics gap (see the "Follow-up diagnostic session" paragraph and `QUESTIONS_FOR_DEVS.md` item 6), got the largest improvement (+0.015) of the four. Winter wheat's slight regression is consistent with, not contradicted by, its own separately-diagnosed anomaly (real reported stress far exceeds what real soil-moisture depletion would explain by any standard function) -- a water-balance-physics refinement was never expected to fix a problem already shown not to be a water-balance-physics problem. Re-ran the standing tillage sanity check (moldboard plow: real yield gain at a nitrogen-limiting rate, ~zero effect at a non-limiting rate) directly against the updated engine and confirmed it still passes (N=50: +0.67 Mg/ha; N=650: 0.0000), confirming the two mechanisms compose without interfering.

Kept the change (real, verified math, a genuine net improvement across three of four crops, and the crop-specific pattern of who improved and who didn't matches this file's own prior diagnosis rather than contradicting it). Ported `campbell_khe()` and the updated `redistribute()` into both embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE` copies identically, confirmed byte-identical to each other afterward (39332 bytes each), and wired `psi_e_kpa`/`B` into every `make_layers()`-equivalent across both files' glue layers (the same 8/2 call sites already carrying `ksat_mm_day` from earlier the same day, one shared find-and-replace per file). Verified the extracted embedded engine's `make_layers()` output carries real `psi_e_kpa`/`B` values and that an isolated `redistribute()` call against it runs cleanly.

Not done, still flagged honestly: this remains a same-day approximation, not the paper's own dynamically-timestepped sub-daily iteration (Eq. 2's travel-time formula, which would recompute khe repeatedly within a single day as each layer's moisture falls, rather than once per day using the day's starting moisture) -- a further, still-larger undertaking if the marginal gains here motivate pursuing it further. `model-validation.html`'s gap-list item 5 updated again to state this specific result (which crops moved which direction and by how much) rather than leaving the prior entry's "not yet attempted" framing standing after it was, in fact, attempted.

Real substepping bug caught in the very fix just shipped, same day, before moving on to anything else: checking whether the single-day Eq. 1 application (just committed) was actually a good approximation of Eq. 2's real sub-daily integration -- rather than assuming "closer to the real equation" automatically meant "good enough" -- found that applying campbell_khe() once per day using only the day's *starting* moisture substantially overdrains, since the real rate keeps falling as the layer drains within the day and a single Euler step never sees that decline. A synthetic near-saturated topsoil layer drained 22.2mm in one step against a converged ~12.2mm once integrated at fine sub-daily resolution (checked by sweeping substep counts from 1 to 480 and confirming convergence) -- roughly 1.8x too much water leaving the layer, a real, substantial error introduced by the very fix meant to make this more accurate, not a minor rounding difference.

Fixed by integrating campbell_khe() across `REDISTRIBUTE_SUBSTEPS=24` (hourly) sub-daily steps per layer per day in `redistribute()`, recomputing the rate each step from that step's own current moisture -- a disclosed, fixed-step approximation of the paper's own adaptive-step-size scheme (Eq. 2 itself defines a travel-time-driven adaptive step, not a fixed hourly grid), not a literal implementation of that adaptive stepping, but a real numerical integration of the same governing rate law instead of one coarse step. 24 was chosen after checking convergence directly: 1.5% off a 480-step reference at 24 steps, vs. 82% off at the single-step version just shipped -- a real, checked tradeoff, not a round-number guess. The two fallback tiers (flat ksat_mm_day cap, original unlimited-rate behavior, both used by any make_layers()-equivalent not yet carrying the full Saxton-Rawls parameter set) are untouched and verified byte-identical to their pre-change behavior.

Re-ran the full validation suite: three of four crops improved further in the same direction the single-step version already showed (corn 0.546->0.549, soybean 0.851->0.854, silage corn 0.526->0.530), winter wheat continued moving slightly the other way (0.222->0.216) -- a consistent, coherent signal across both rounds of this fix, not noise, though still a small movement overall rather than a breakthrough. The standing tillage sanity check (moldboard plow: real yield gain at a nitrogen-limiting rate, exactly zero at a non-limiting rate) was re-verified directly against the substepped engine and still passes. Ported into both embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE` copies identically, confirmed byte-identical to each other afterward (40023 bytes each), and confirmed the extracted embedded engine's substepped drainage and both fallback tiers all behave correctly. `model-validation.html`'s gap-list item 5 updated a third time with this specific result.

Real FAO-56 two-stage evaporation-reduction coefficient implemented, replacing bare-soil evaporation's "no disclosed formula, standard proxy" status for the first time this week (2026-09-23, continuing the same day's thread): only Chapter 8 (water stress) had ever been supplied as a full document, matching this file's own already-disclosed gap for bare-soil/residue evaporation (item 2 in `model-validation.html`'s gap list). Rather than ask Matt for another chapter immediately, tried WebSearch first this round and it worked well enough: recovered FAO-56's real Eq. 74 (Kr, the two-stage evaporation-reduction coefficient) and Eq. 73 (TEW, total evaporable water) verbatim from search summaries quoting the paper directly, both independently checkable against real, well-known formulas (Eq. 73 in particular is a widely-cited textbook expression, TEW=1000*(theta_fc-0.5*theta_wp)*Ze). REW (readily evaporable water, a soil-texture-dependent Table 19 lookup) couldn't be recovered as an exact per-texture table -- used a single disclosed default (9mm, the middle of the real, confirmed 5-12mm range) rather than inventing texture-specific numbers from an unconfirmed table.

Scoped deliberately short of FAO-56's full dual crop coefficient method: only the two-stage depletion mechanism (Kr, driven by a new persistent per-season `de_state` tracking cumulative depletion since the surface was last wetted) was implemented, not the method's own Kcmax-based potential-demand term (Eq. 72, which needs a basal crop coefficient Kcb this engine doesn't compute, since it grows canopy via a different Campbell-style radiation/transpiration mechanism, not FAO-56's own Kc*ETo framework). The potential-demand term itself (`eto_mm*(1-canopy_cover_frac)`) is unchanged, still this engine's own pre-existing proxy -- a disclosed, bounded piece of the real method, not a full replacement, the same kind of partial-but-real fix already applied to tillage's decomposition boost and the water-redistribution physics this same day.

Verified in isolation before wiring into the full engine: with `de_state=None` (the default), `soil_evaporation()` reproduces the exact prior behavior; with a real de_state, a synthetic 10-day dry-down test showed evaporation proceeding at the full potential rate through Stage 1, then decelerating smoothly once cumulative depletion exceeded REW (Stage 2) -- a real, qualitatively different behavior from the old proxy, which never slowed down as long as *some* water remained above wilting point. Wired `de_state` through all four `soil_evaporation()` call sites in `simulate_season()`/`_reference_n_demand()` (initialized once per call from `compute_tew()` on the top layer's own fc/pwp and `REW_DEFAULT_MM`), plus `bare_fallow_leaching()` in `engine-demo.html`'s own demo glue -- the same "keep the counterfactual fair" fix already made once for runoff on this exact function: leaving the rotation panel's bare-fallow comparison on the old no-memory evaporation while the real crop phases used the new depletion-aware version would have made fallow soil evaporate unrealistically fast relative to a real crop's own bare-soil days, understating fallow leaching relative to the cover crop.

Ran the full validation suite: **zero** measurable effect on any of the four validated crops, to three decimal places. Diagnosed directly rather than left unexplained: checked the actual depletion trajectory over Rock Springs' driest early-season 60 days and found `de` never once exceeds `REW=9mm` -- real rainfall resets it too often at this site for Stage 2 to ever trigger. This is the fourth real, correctly-implemented mechanism in a row (after curve-number runoff, antecedent-moisture spin-up, and the Ksat-based rate limits) to be non-load-bearing specifically because Rock Springs' humid climate never stresses it, not because the formula is wrong -- kept anyway since it's real, sourced, and could matter at a drier site where the surface genuinely dries out between rain events. Re-verified the standing tillage sanity check still passes. Ported into both embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE` copies identically (confirmed byte-identical to each other afterward, 41202 bytes each) and into `bare_fallow_leaching()` in `engine-demo.html` specifically (not part of the canonical script). `model-validation.html`'s gap-list item 2 updated to state what's now resolved (the two-stage mechanism, verified) versus what remains open (Cycles' own real evaporation formula, still undisclosed; the exact REW table; FAO-56's own Kcmax refinement, not attempted).

"What would a real modeler do" extended to the remaining gap list, then acted on (2026-09-23): asked directly whether the four remaining undisclosed-formula items (fwc, the pasture cutting trigger, wheat's anomaly, ammonia volatilization) should be pursued via broader literature rather than more Cycles-specific guessing. Pushed back on treating this as one uniform strategy rather than agreeing outright: ammonia volatilization is a well-studied *general* soil-science process with real external literature independent of Cycles (the right target for a literature search); fwc is a narrower search within the same SWAT-family documentation that already yielded real AMC-I/III formulas; the pasture trigger needs a different framing (build an independently-sourced agronomic rule, not reverse-engineer Cycles' undisclosed internal one); wheat's anomaly needed a five-minute check of already-available data (whether `GenericCrops.crop` gives wheat a real per-species root-growth-rate difference this engine's shared generic root curve ignores) before any new search, not literature at all. Matt asked to pursue ammonia volatilization first.

Real ammonia volatilization pathway implemented (2026-09-23, same day): WebSearch first surfaced EPIC's own "enhanced" volatilization submodel, but it turned out to be a full mechanistic system (hourly NH4+/NH3 equilibrium partitioning plus urea hydrolysis kinetics) -- the same category of overreach as the full six-pool carbon system already correctly ruled out of v1 scope, not a tractable single-function addition. A follow-up search for a specific applied/empirical alternative (Macnack, Chim & Raun 2013, a real published linear temperature/pH/wind-speed model built specifically for growers considering surface urea) found the right paper but not its exact coefficients (likely paywalled beyond the abstract) -- logged as a real candidate for later if Matt can supply the PDF the way Chapter 8 was supplied, not pursued further this round.

What was actually implemented instead: real IPCC 2006/2019 Refinement Tier-1 default emission factors (Table 11.3, a widely-cited general standard, not a single narrow study) -- `NH3_FRAC_SYNTHETIC=0.10` (10% of applied synthetic fertilizer N volatilizes when surface-broadcast) and `NH3_FRAC_MANURE=0.20` (organic amendments volatilize at roughly twice the mineral-fertilizer rate). This pairs with real data this engine already has rather than needing anything new: `TILLAGE_IMPLEMENTS`' own per-implement `mixing_efficiency` (parsed from Cycles' real till.txt, already used for tillage's soil-mixing effect) is reused for the physically real reason that incorporating fertilizer (an anhydrous knife, a manure injector) shields it from atmospheric loss the way surface broadcast never does -- `effective_loss_fraction = base_fraction * (1 - mixing_efficiency)`, a disclosed reinterpretation of a real number for a second real physical effect of the same operation, the same pattern already used once for tillage itself.

New `fert_placement_implement` parameter on `simulate_season()` (None by default, reproducing the exact prior zero-volatilization behavior): when set to a real implement name, reduces the mineral fertilizer and manure amounts actually entering the nitrogen pool by their respective volatilization fractions, before either affects yield (the whole-season adequacy fraction) or leaching (the day-by-day pool). `n_credit_kg_ha` (a previous-crop residual, not a fresh surface application) is deliberately excluded from this mechanism. A new `n_volatilized_kg_ha` output field completes the mass balance alongside the existing uptake/leached/remaining fields.

Verified directly: a 150 kg N/ha broadcast application (`Fert_applic_surface_broadcast`, real mixing_efficiency=0) volatilizes exactly 15.0 kg (150*0.10); the same rate through `Fert_applic_anhyd_knife_12_in` (real mixing_efficiency=0.265919) volatilizes 11.01 kg (150*0.10*(1-0.265919)) -- both exact matches to the formula, not approximate. Yield under injection (7.773 Mg/ha) is measurably higher than under broadcast (7.666 Mg/ha) at the same nominal 150 kg N/ha rate, Rock Springs 2012 -- the real, physically expected direction (Matt's own stated interest: injection reduces volatilization loss). Mass balance closes exactly (uptake+leached+remaining+volatilized = total N supply, checked to three decimals) for both cases. The manure-availability equivalence sanity check (200 kg manure at real 0.5 availability = 100 kg mineral) still holds exactly when no placement implement is given, confirming the new mechanism doesn't interfere with the existing one when unused. An unrecognized implement name still raises `ValueError`. Ran the full validation suite: all four crops' correlations unchanged to three decimals (the harnesses never pass `fert_placement_implement`), confirming this is purely additive. Re-verified the standing tillage sanity check (moldboard plow: real yield gain at a nitrogen-limiting rate, zero at a non-limiting rate) composes correctly alongside the new mechanism.

Ported into both embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE` copies identically, confirmed byte-identical to each other afterward (42604 bytes each), and confirmed the extracted embedded engine reproduces the exact same volatilized amounts (15.0 broadcast, 11.011 injected) through its own `simulate_season()`. `model-validation.html`'s gap-list item 8 rewritten to state what's resolved (a real, sourced loss pathway now exists) versus what remains open (Cycles' own real formula, still undisclosed; the more precise Macnack et al. model, not yet obtained). Deliberately not wired into any panel's UI yet -- matching the "engine first, UI later" pattern already used for the six mechanisms added 2026-09-17, not an oversight; `model-validation.html`'s own "Full simulation controls" form (which otherwise exposes every `simulate_season()` parameter) is the natural next place to add a fertilizer-placement dropdown if this is picked up again, reusing the same curated-implement-list pattern already used there for tillage.

The Macnack et al. 2013 paper itself found in this repo's own committed files, not paywalled after all (2026-09-23, same day): asked to look for "the paper in the repo (main branch) that says something about ammonia" -- Matt had independently obtained and committed the exact paper this file had just flagged as a real, better-fitting candidate whose coefficients "appear to be paywalled beyond the abstract" (`ammonia paper_rev6.pdf`, committed directly to `main`, the same pattern used for every other blocked source this project has needed). Read in full: Macnack, Chim & Raun, "Applied Model for Estimating Potential Ammonia Loss from Surface Applied Urea" (Communications in Soil Science and Plant Analysis 44:2055-2063, Oklahoma State University). A real composite built from 159 records / 43 site-years across 25 published articles (1960-2010), specific to surface-applied (broadcast) urea, not incorporated fertilizer or manure: three separate linear regressions (ammonia loss % vs. soil pH, wind speed, air temperature -- each individually significant at p<0.05 but with low r^2: 0.18, 0.27, 0.04 respectively, Table 1, a real disclosed limitation of a composite built across studies with different measurement windows) combined additively into one final equation using the base model with the largest effect size (pH) plus the other two variables' own slopes: AL(%) = -40.7 + 8.43(pH) + 3.85(WS, m/sec) + 0.33(AT, deg C). The paper's own online calculator additionally asks for presence/absence of surface residue, but that variable's own coefficient was never reported (a multiple regression across all four variables lacked sufficient degrees of freedom, stated directly in the paper) -- so residue effects are not part of the disclosed, implementable equation.

Implemented as `macnack_ammonia_loss_pct(soil_ph, air_temp_c, wind_speed_ms)` in `cycles_engine_validate.py`, returning a fraction clamped to [0,1] (the linear form is otherwise unbounded outside the real data's range). Wired into `simulate_season()` as a new opt-in `soil_ph=None` parameter that, when given, REPLACES the flat IPCC NH3_FRAC_SYNTHETIC=0.10 default with this real, weather-driven per-application estimate -- looking up each mineral application's own real day (mean tx/tn for air temp, that day's real wind) from `weather_rows`, data this engine already carries, not a new dependency. This required restructuring the single flat `mineral_retention` scalar (shipped hours earlier the same day) into a `mineral_retention_for_doy(doy)` closure, since retention now genuinely varies by application day when `soil_ph` is given, while collapsing to the exact prior flat-scalar behavior when it isn't (verified: `soil_ph=None` reproduces the just-shipped IPCC-only numbers to full float precision, both the plain-broadcast and incorporated-implement cases). Manure keeps the flat IPCC 20% default regardless of `soil_ph` -- Macnack's data is urea-specific, not manure -- and the existing implement mixing-efficiency reduction still composes on top of whichever base rate is active (flat IPCC or Macnack), since Macnack's own equation says nothing about incorporation and doesn't contradict reusing that same real per-implement number for it.

Real, disclosed limitation surfaced immediately on testing, not glossed over: at Rock Springs' real 2012 planting-day weather (warm, breezy spring day) with a typical cropland pH default of 6.5, the model estimates a striking 37% loss on a broadcast application (56.07 of 150 kg N/ha) -- much higher than the flat IPCC 10% default, and not independently checked against a real field measurement for this exact site/day (the paper's own low r^2 values mean this composite carries real, disclosed uncertainty). This isn't necessarily wrong (real field studies do report ammonia losses in this range under warm/windy conditions, per the literature review's own cited numbers), but it's a genuinely different order of magnitude from the flat default, worth knowing before trusting any specific number from it. `soil_ph` itself has no real data source in this engine at all -- STATSGO2 carries no pH field, so it's caller-supplied, not derived, same honest-gap treatment as every other undisclosed/unavailable input in this file.

Verified: the formula reproduces the paper's own coefficients exactly by hand calculation; clamping confirmed at a synthetic extreme-low input (pH 4, -5 deg C, calm -> 0.0); `soil_ph=None` reproduces the just-shipped IPCC-only path byte-for-byte in both the plain-broadcast and incorporated-implement cases; a real per-application-day lookup confirmed working for a two-event split application at different real weather days; mass balance still closes exactly (uptake+leached+remaining+volatilized = supply, checked to 4 decimals) with `soil_ph` active; manure volatilization unaffected by `soil_ph` (still exactly the flat 20%); the standing tillage sanity check (moldboard plow: real gain at a nitrogen-limiting rate, zero at a non-limiting rate) still passes unchanged; the full four-crop validation suite (`run_validation.py`/`run_validation_rotation2.py`) reproduces the exact same correlations as before this change (corn 0.549, soybean 0.854, wheat 0.216, silage corn 0.530 -- neither harness passes `soil_ph`, confirming this is purely additive). Ported into both embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE` copies identically, confirmed byte-identical to each other afterward (43965 bytes each) and confirmed via the same isolated-CPython-execution checks as the canonical script. `model-validation.html`'s gap-list item 8 updated with the full account, including the honest 37%-at-Rock-Springs-2012 finding stated plainly rather than smoothed over, and the still-open items (no real per-site pH data, low r^2, undisclosed measurement windows, no UI wiring anywhere). Headless-Chromium check confirmed both HTML files still load cleanly with unchanged panel/gap-list counts and no new console errors beyond the standing CDN-unreachable ones.

`soil_ph` wired into the field-comparison and nitrogen-sweep/frontier panels' UI (2026-09-23, same day, per Matt's direct ask), engine-demo.html only -- model-validation.html's own "Full simulation controls" form already exposes `simulate_season()`'s raw parameters directly and needed no UI addition. Followed the exact pattern already established for tillage in both panels: a curated `SOIL_PH_OPTIONS` array (Not modeled / 5.5 acidic / 6.5 neutral-typical / 7.5 slightly alkaline / 8.5 alkaline) backing a shared `buildSoilPhFieldHTML(id)` template, rather than a raw numeric input -- this engine has no real per-site pH data (STATSGO2 carries none) to ground a free-entry field against, so a small set of real, labeled values a student can reason about beats an unanchored slider. "" (Not modeled, the default) keeps `soil_ph=None`, byte-identical to this page's pre-existing behavior, same convention as tillage's "No tillage" default.

Nitrogen-sweep panel: the pH select sits directly below the existing tillage select, its own explanatory line describing ammonia loss as a separate pathway from leaching (nitrogen lost to the air, never reaching the water OR the crop). Wired into the per-rate `simulate_season()` calls inside the sweep loop; each sweep row now also carries `n_volatilized_kg_ha`. A new bar chart + note pair (mirroring the existing leaching chart exactly) shows volatilized nitrogen across the 14 swept rates, always rendered for corn (even at "Not modeled," where it correctly shows zero bars and a note pointing at the pH selector) and hidden for soybean alongside the other nitrogen-only elements, since soybean never tracks nitrogen at all regardless of `soil_ph`. Left the profit/frontier/three-perspectives calculations untouched -- they're driven by yield and leaching, not volatilization, and ammonia loss reducing effective nitrogen supply already shows up correctly through those two derived numbers without needing separate wiring.

Field-comparison panel: the pH select sits next to the shared tillage select (both explicitly shared across Field A/B, not per-field, for the same reason tillage already is -- avoiding a second confound alongside the location comparison). Wired into `runFieldSlot()`'s Python body; the function's existing `**_res` dict-spread already forwards `n_volatilized_kg_ha` to the caller with no changes needed there. Added a fourth mini bar-chart column (yield/AG biomass/leached/volatilized, grid widened from 3 to 4 columns) and a matching "Ammonia volatilized (kg N/ha)" table row; the result note now names the selected pH option alongside tillage practice.

Verified in headless Chromium with a mocked `pyodide` (the real Python logic was already verified when `soil_ph` was added to the engine itself; this check is purely the JS wiring): both selects render the correct 5 options defaulting to "Not modeled"; the nitrogen-sweep panel's default run shows the volatilized chart with a zero-note, and selecting pH 6.5 correctly sets the `run_soil_ph` global to "6.5" and switches to the real explanatory note text; the field-comparison panel correctly sets `field_soil_ph` to "7.5" on run, the result table gains the new volatilized row, and the fourth chart element exists. Confirmed both HTML files' `ENGINE_SOURCE` strings are untouched and still byte-identical to each other (this was an outer-page-glue-only change in engine-demo.html; model-validation.html wasn't touched at all). A full-page panel count (13) confirmed unchanged, and screenshots of both new controls confirm clean rendering matching the established visual language.

"Keep working through gaps as modelers, no caveat needed" (2026-09-23, per Matt's direct instruction after the soil-pH UI wiring shipped): continued the same real-formula-sourcing discipline rather than adding a hedge to the new ammonia-loss chart. Two threads pursued, one a real dead end worth recording, one a real fix.

**Wheat's water-stress anomaly, tested via real LWP data -- dead end, not pursued further.** Matt's own local Cycles container (this session's `/tmp/cycles-run/`, persisted from earlier work) turned out to still have the real `GenericCrops.crop` file, which carries `LWP_STRESS_ONSET`/`LWP_WILTING_POINT` (leaf water potential thresholds, J/kg) per crop -- real, disclosed, per-species data never previously used in this engine's water-stress function (`water_stress_response()` currently uses FAO-56's generic depletion-fraction approach instead). Hypothesis: converting root-zone soil moisture to a matric potential via the already-computed Saxton-Rawls Campbell-curve parameters (`psi_e_kpa`/`B`), then comparing directly against these real per-crop LWP thresholds, might explain wheat's specific anomaly (real reported stress far exceeds what mild soil-moisture depletion would predict by any standard function -- see the "Follow-up diagnostic session" paragraph earlier in this file). Tested numerically before touching any code, the same discipline used for every formula in this file: computed the soil-moisture value at which each crop's real LWP_STRESS_ONSET/LWP_WILTING_POINT would be crossed, using both the full psi_e/B Campbell curve and a fc/pwp-anchored variant. Both gave the same wrong-direction answer -- stress onset landing at avail_frac 0.02-0.16 (near-total depletion) for ALL of corn/soybean/wheat, barely differentiated between crops, and often unreachable at all within the engine's existing pwp extraction floor. This is the OPPOSITE of the documented real finding (wheat stresses at avail_frac ~0.83, mild depletion) -- confirming Saxton-Rawls' own psi_e isn't well-calibrated to reproduce the standard -33kPa/-1500kPa reference points it's nominally anchored to (checked directly: psi_mag(fc)=52kPa not 33, psi_mag(pwp)=2366kPa not 1500, a known real messiness in the Saxton-Rawls system, not a bug in this file's own derivation). Conclusion: these LWP thresholds most likely require the actual undisclosed Campbell/Jara-Stockle root-conductance network (leaf potential is driven below soil potential by the flow rate needed to meet transpiration demand, not readable directly off a soil-moisture curve) -- not pursued further this round, a real, evidence-backed dead end rather than a silently abandoned idea.

**The `fwc` curve-number moisture-adjustment factor -- resolved with a real, sourced formula.** Pivoted to this instead, per the earlier "narrower SWAT-family search" plan (this file's own `cn_dry()`/`cn_wet()` AMC formulas already came from SWAT+ documentation, so a parallel search made sense). WebSearch surfaced SWAT's own real, exact soil-moisture-based retention-parameter equation (Neitsch et al., SWAT theoretical documentation, Eq. 2:1.1.11-2:1.1.13) -- WebFetch itself is blocked for every SWAT-hosting domain tried (swat.tamu.edu, swatplus.gitbook.io, bubalis.github.io), so the equation came from search-result summaries, not a direct primary-source read, same standing limitation as everywhere else in this file. `S = Smax*(1 - SW/(SW+exp(w1-w2*SW)))`, with `w1`/`w2` solved from three real anchor points: Smax (the retention parameter at CN1/dry, an asymptote as SW->0), S3 (at CN3/wet, anchored at SW=field-capacity -- SWAT's own real convention that field capacity represents the "wet" runoff condition, not saturation), and a CN=99 anchor at SW=saturation. SW/FC/SAT are all profile-wide totals (the WHOLE soil column, not the earlier ad hoc top-0.6m-depth-weighted guess `compute_fwc()` used).

Verified before trusting it, not from the search summary alone: reproduced all three of the formula's own defining anchor points to full float precision at Rock Springs' real soil profile and CN2=75 (S(SW->0)=Smax=192.69mm exactly, S(SW=FC)=S3=32.22mm exactly, S(SW=SAT)=S-at-CN99=2.57mm exactly), and confirmed the curve is smoothly, monotonically decreasing as SW rises across the full range -- matching the real, disclosed direction. Implemented as `retention_param_mm(layers, curve_number)` in `cycles_engine_validate.py`, replacing `compute_fwc()`/`moisture_adjusted_cn()` entirely (both deleted, not just superseded); `runoff_mm()`'s signature changed from taking a curve number to taking the retention parameter S directly (mm), with the existing real slope adjustment (`slope_factor()`, unchanged) still applied on top. `infiltrate()` updated to call the new function; no other call site needed changes, since `infiltrate()` was already the single shared entry point for both the main season loop and the optional spin-up window.

Ran the full four-crop validation suite: correlations moved by single-thousandths in both directions (corn 0.549->0.546, soybean 0.854->0.857, wheat 0.216->0.223, silage corn 0.530->0.523) -- small and mixed, not a breakthrough, but kept anyway since it's a real, better-sourced, more mechanistically grounded formula, the same standard already applied to today's other water-balance refinements (runoff/spin-up, the Ksat rate cap) that also moved little or nothing. Re-verified the standing tillage sanity check (moldboard plow: real yield gain at a nitrogen-limiting rate, zero at a non-limiting rate) still passes. Ported into both embedded `ENGINE_SOURCE` copies identically, confirmed byte-identical to each other afterward (43994 bytes each) and confirmed the extracted embedded engine reproduces the canonical script's exact reference number (2012 corn @ N=150, no tillage: 8.031091501200546 Mg/ha, matched to full float precision). `model-validation.html`'s gap-list item 1 updated from "declined by devs" to "resolved," and `QUESTIONS_FOR_DEVS.md`'s item 1 moved from "Open" to "Resolved without asking" (the item number itself retired rather than the list renumbered, since several other items are cross-referenced by number elsewhere in this file).

Real, disclosed `TRANSPIRATION_MAX` wired in -- a genuine no-op at Rock Springs, kept for other sites (2026-09-23, continuing the "move onto the next fix" thread after the `fwc` resolution): before reaching for something new, checked what real per-crop data in `GenericCrops.crop` this engine still wasn't using at all. Found `TRANSPIRATION_MAX` (a physical daily-transpiration ceiling: corn/silage corn 10 mm/day, soybean/wheat 8 mm/day) -- present in the crop file, never read anywhere in this engine. Checked before implementing whether it could plausibly matter: computed `TRp` (potential transpiration) across the full 37-year Rock Springs record and found it never exceeds ~7.4mm/day for corn, comfortably under even the tightest real cap -- so this was very likely to be another null result at this specific site, the same pattern as today's earlier runoff/spin-up/Ksat-rate-cap work.

Implemented anyway as `crop.get("tr_max_mm_day", math.inf)`, capping `TR_actual` right after the existing water-stress reduction, at both call sites (`_reference_n_demand()` and the main `simulate_season()` loop) -- a graceful no-op default for any crop dict that doesn't set the field.
